# Changelog

All notable changes to Ferrum are documented here.

## Unreleased

Wave-1 hardening closes 24 issues (#92, #107, #118, #122, #126, #133, #136, #140, #148, #152, #162, #166, #179, #183, #190, #197, #198, #204, #208, #216, #220, #238, #240, #243) under one standing policy: **refuse malformed, warn degraded**. Every reported silent accept/degrade becomes a typed refusal or a named warning; four changes are disclosed as user-visible behavior changes (see Breaking, below); everything else is additive (a new error message, a new `UserWarning`) with no other rendered geometry moving.

Wave-2 break-axis geometry closes four more issues (#226, #228, #230, #231). #226 fixes the root pixel-range defect that silently discarded a broken axis's own scale padding on every effective break, and the tick-label rigid-translation fix that closes the label↔dash offset discrepancy it exposed. #228 unifies the annotation-clip contract with mark geometry: an extent annotation now clips into visible pieces instead of being suppressed whole the moment a gap touches it. #230 closes nine disclosed residuals of the pixel-space surgery model (same-axis merge, hidden-sentinel poisoning, per-segment vertex splitting, split-corner squaring, exact glyph anchors, split-key suffixing, per-facet-panel application, typed Polar/Geo refusal, and temporal gap bounds). #231 lands the discriminating coverage that pins all of it — an arrowed-segment golden plus e2e test, a tick-label-offset assertion, a CoordFlip physical-axis characterization test, and faceted-annotation coverage — with no behavior change of its own.

Wave-3 diagnostics sklearn-parity closes the pre-batch-I campaign's eleven wave-3 issues (#189, #203, #206, #209, #211, #212, #213, #217, #219, #222, #224 — the last of these in two parts, #224a and #224b) plus five research-confirmed findings (N1–N5), making the model-diagnostics layer's target handling, class resolution, cross-validation, `compare=` composition, embeddings validation, caching, and metric display truthful and sklearn-consistent. #203 is the first of its issues: the answer a corrupted classification target used to get depended on the target's dtype and on which resolution tier the model happened to land in, and every branch of that fork ended silently or raggedly. One matrix now covers the seven `ModelSource` classification methods and the eight classification methods of the precomputed path — nulls refuse, stray values warn (naming what actually happens to the rows: absorbed as negative on a curve, dropped from the matrix on a confusion matrix), and the total-mismatch refusal reaches the two builders and the one arity it never had. #206 and #224a close the two remaining `classification_report` gaps: the model path now translates `model.predict` into `y`'s domain through the same correspondence the confusion fix built (closing the one classification surface #203 left outside the target-hygiene contract) and both `classification_report_chart` paths now order the class axis identically. Clean targets are byte-identical and no golden moved. #209 closes the multiclass partial-dependence gap: `ModelSource.partial_dependence()` kept only sklearn's `[0]` output for a multi-class classifier, silently discarding every other class's curve; it now emits one row group per class with a `class_label` column, mirroring the convention `shap_values()` already used — and `shap_values()` itself gains the one case neither method had answered, numbering a `classes_`-less multi-output regressor's outputs instead of labelling them all `"target"`. #211 (with N2 and N3) restores two spec claims that had quietly gone false: `discrimination_threshold(cv=<int>)`'s hand-rolled `KFold` shuffled with `random_state`, so "ignores `random_state`" was no longer true, and a training fold could land with only one class and no warning. It now resolves via sklearn's own unshuffled `StratifiedKFold` convention — no seed to thread, so the claim is true again — and warns by name when a training fold is left with one class. #213 closes the wave's `compare=` composition gap: `discrimination_threshold_chart` was accepting `compare=` and dropping the model column at unpivot, so the models it was asked to compare rendered as one interleaved zig-zag under a single pooled optimum — it now composes small multiples, one panel per model, each panel built by re-entering the single-model builder so its optimum and threshold rule are per-model by construction. #212 closes the last `compare=` gap in the classification visualizer family: `CalibrationVisualizer` accepted a symmetric `*models` overlay but refused `compare=` outright, so the one multi-model shape it offered was also the one that reports no `.score()` and no single headline number — asking it to measure one model against rivals meant giving up the base model's own answer. It now accepts both idioms, for the two different questions they ask. #222 (with N4) closes the embeddings perplexity gap: `.embeddings(method="tsne")`'s default forwarded unclamped to manifolds-rs, so the *unmodified default* refused on every dataset of 31 rows or fewer; it now adapts to `min(30, n_samples - 2)`, and an explicit out-of-range or non-finite value refuses by name instead of leaking an internal message or panicking the PyO3 boundary. #224b (with N5) closes the cache gap the same research pass confirmed: `BaseSource._cache_key`'s `repr()` fallback could collide two logically different calls onto the same cached frame (a cv splitter's reused memory address, a truncated numpy repr, a truncated `shap_values(background=)` string) — it now normalizes array-likes centrally and never caches a value that stays unhashable after that. #189 closes a narrower gap on the same mark #213 touches: an *explicit* `mark_discrimination_threshold(optimum_label=True)`/`(threshold_line=True)` used to vanish silently when `metrics=` excluded `"f1"`; it now refuses, naming the flag and `metrics=`, while the un-passed default keeps its pre-existing silent downgrade. #219 settles which surface owns a metric: `annotate_auc`/`annotate_ap`/`annotate_brier` drove three surfaces at once — the chart title, the legend entry and the endpoint overlay — so a three-class ROC printed each class's AUC three times, and the title printed it whether or not the flag was set, which meant `annotate_*=False` never actually took the number off the figure. The overlay now owns it alone, the titles keep their plain names, and the flag means what it says. The remaining research finding, N1, closes alongside #217, the pyarrow-table asymmetry the same research pass was investigating: #217 as filed turned out to be a phantom, already fixed by wave 1's `545be19a`, and closes at origins on that evidence rather than on anything in this wave — but underneath it, N1 found and fixed a live, deeper defect in the same code path: a multi-chunk `pyarrow.Table` silently truncated to its first chunk on the raw-data rank path (measured on a 200-row fixture split into two chunks: `variance` moved from 1.169347 to the correct 1.018316).

Wave-4 figure helpers closes ten issues (#90, #121, #130, #131, #192, #193, #194, #199, #200, #201 — the last in two parts, #201-B and #201-D) plus a zero-extent-rect rider in the bar renderer. The theme is a parameter that was accepted and then not consulted: a channel's `title=` reached axes but no legend (#90), `bins=` reached a door that read only integers and silently fell back to Sturges (#193), `multiple="stack"` reached `displot`'s own position adjustment but never the binning that makes a stack possible (#194), `line_kws` reached a rewrite that ran after the layers it meant to style existed (#199), and the `hue=` column reached an ecdf `Bin` that had already thrown it away (#200). Three more are cases of the wrong question being asked: eligibility for the single-axis exemption asked the composed spec's placeholder root mark rather than the marks that draw (#201-D), the `hue=` typing helper asked whether the caller passed an object rather than whether they stated a type (#130), and `catplot(kind="count")` asked the raw argument what its seven siblings ask the resolved channel (#192). #121 takes the iso-line grouping key off the `color` channel it was missing against and puts it on `detail`, so the reference curves finally wear the grey they always declared. #131 adds no behavior: it puts `mark_tick` into the mark×dtype color-parity sweep it was missing from, so the read batch E gave it cannot quietly go away again. Nine of the ten move user-visible output; every one is disclosed under Breaking, below, with the measured before/after. The wave's closing review added a second round of user-visible change on top: `multiple="dodge"` now bins on shared edges and subdivides its band (which reaches every dodged span mark, not only `displot`, and closes a silently dropped bar along the way), `displot` answers in its own voice instead of leaking `mark_histogram()`'s, the fit paths accept an encoding object on every method, and an all-zero-extent bar batch says why it drew nothing. Those are disclosed under Breaking too.

Wave-5 coordinate, scale and mark fidelity closes eleven issues (#95, #111, #113, #124, #153, #171, #172 — in three parts, #172(1)/(2)/(3) — #182, #184, #185, #247 in two parts, #247A and #247B) plus #94, which #182's gate closes with it, and three fixes added mid-wave that no issue named. The theme is a fact that was **inferred where it should have been declared**. A bar's zero-anchored scale was indistinguishable from one the caller wrote, so an overlaid bar bypassed the cross-mark domain union and drew against a domain its own axis did not describe (#247A). Two mark drawers inferred the value axis from a y-axis idiom that `CoordFlip` invalidates, so a flipped area closed its fill on the wrong edge and a flipped ribbon drew nothing at all (#172(2)/(3)). A sort shorthand's channel token was read in rendering space while the caller wrote it in authored space, so `sort="-y"` stopped working the moment a flip was added (#95). Layout inferred that a hidden axis still needed its gutter (#182), a composite inferred which panels were marginals from the grid's shape, and a layered chart inferred its ordinal axis from the chart-level column alone. Four things are consequently now **declared on the wire** — `Layer.coord`, `MarkKwargsSpec::value_axis`, `ScaleSpec::Linear::system_injected`, and `CompositeNode::Composite::extent_alignments` — each additive and skip-when-absent, each proven byte-stable by round-tripping a pre-change payload, and each landing with the heuristic it replaces **deleted** rather than left beside it. Alongside them: a declared or defaulted category channel now bands whatever its dtype (#171 + #185), the three position adjustments agree on which dtypes they can read and **a stacked time series renders where it used to raise** (#184), `nice=True` on a typed scale class stops being discarded (#153), a legend swatch shows the encoding it stands for (#111), a band exists only if something is drawn in it (#247B), and the shared ordinal axis covers every layer's categories so a layered chart stops silently dropping marks (a user ruling, added mid-wave). That last one is also where the wave's closing review found it had **overclaimed**: the ordinal ladder read the CHART's flip for every layer while its numeric sibling read each layer's own, so `#172`(1) was honored on continuous axes and still silently dropping marks on categorical ones. Both halves are closed now — the ladder reads each layer's own coord, and the member a shared panel genuinely cannot hold refuses loudly instead of rendering without it — and the corrected scope is stated in the `#172`(1) entries below rather than left implied. Every user-visible output change is disclosed under Breaking, below, with the measured before/after.

Wave-6A structural hygiene works through ten issues (#105, #110, #145, #146, #147, #158, #163, #196, #229, #237). Most of it is internal and byte-stable by proof: four modules past the repo's own promotion trigger get job-named homes, and four pieces of untyped state (a string sentinel, a conflated integer, four parallel booleans, a stringly axis) become types. The wave's research found **four live defects** inside what was filed as hygiene, each named under Fixed: a cleared paint lost to the color channel (#110), an interactive keyed transition wrote onto the wrong instances whenever chrome shared the mark buffers (#105), and an independent-x facet axis ignored `Theme(cull_threshold=)` (#146) and dropped a temporal tick format (#147). The deliberate output changes are listed under Breaking, and the module moves under Internal. `#229`(4) is closed WONTFIX-by-design (see Internal).

Wave-6B single-home dedup and rule ownership works through eleven issues (#119, #123, #128, #137, #149, #164, #165 in four parts, #176 (one item), #195, #214, #239); #218 is closed on evidence already in the tree. Each rule that was written in more than one place gets one owner (Inset bounds classification, the `compare=` merge, the aggregate vocabulary, the secondary-key rule, the registry-consumer lint), and the byte-stable half of that is listed under Internal. The wave's research found **three live defects** inside what was filed as dedup: a layer's style leaked into kwarg-less sibling layers (#119, listed under Breaking because it changes rendered output on purpose), `Shape(scale=)` was inert so `pairplot(markers=)` ignored its argument (#123), and a string conditional literal on a numeric channel repainted the mark's fill on selection (#128). Inputs that were accepted and did nothing, or were silently laundered, now refuse by name under the same **refuse malformed, warn degraded** policy as wave 1; each such refusal is listed under Breaking. The aggregate vocabulary's three surface changes are disclosed as one entry. No golden moved.

Wave-6C's disclosed changes, below, are `#120`(6)'s numeric-door unification, `#120`(7)/N2's auxiliary-warning-count fix, `#120`(9)/N1's layer shared-axis refusal, `#139`(2)(3)(4)'s `padding.auto` hardening, `#159`'s `ScaleSpec` tag-vocabulary publication, `#159`(10)'s override-propagation fix, `#167`(1)'s `Utf8View`/`LargeUtf8` normalization, `#176`(7)'s violin hue warning, `#176`(8)'s `Layer(transforms=)` refusal, `#176`(10a)(10b)/N5's facet-state merge, `#176`(11)/N3's `Facet(sort=)` fix, `#245`(3)'s conditional-collision message, N4's `BreakAxisIneffective` domain-order fix, and F19/F19b's interactive-dash fix. Also in this wave: `#221` (diagnostic parameter-forwarding test coverage) and `#235` (a headless-Chrome pixel harness for conditional encodings).

### Added

- **A layer carries its own coordinate system, on the wire (#172(1)).** `Layer` gains an additive `coord` slot (skip-when-absent), and `Chart + Chart` — hence `LayerChart`, `.interactive()`, and `resolve={"y": "independent"}`, which all merge through it — carries each secondary member's own coord onto the layers it contributes instead of dropping it. `fm.layer(bar, arc_chart.coord(fm.CoordPolar(theta="x")))` now renders the arc member's wedges on **both** output kinds rather than refusing on the interactive one, and `fm.layer(bar, point_chart.coord(fm.CoordFlip()))` renders the point member **flipped** rather than silently unflipped, which was the half of the defect that produced no diagnostic at all. A layer's coord places that layer's marks; the panel's chrome, aspect ratio and scale domains stay the chart-level coord's. So what ships is: **honored where the panel can hold the member, refused loudly where it cannot** — on both output kinds, and never a silently different chart. A per-layer coord is unplaceable in two ways, and both refuse with the same typed `ValueError` naming the layer and the coord:

    * it declares a **panel** property instead of a placement (`xlim=`/`ylim=`, `expand=False`, `clip=False`, `CoordFixed(ratio=)`) — `layer 1: fm.CoordFixed declares a fixed aspect ratio ... which belongs to the panel every layer shares`;
    * or its channel exchange lands a column on an axis that cannot draw it — `layer 1: fm.CoordFlip leaves this layer's positional channels in the opposite space from the panel's axes, and the panel's y axis is continuous while the column that lands on it ('cat': Utf8) cannot be drawn against a continuous axis`. This is `fm.layer(bar, point.coord(fm.CoordFlip()))` over a shared `x="cat:N", y="v:Q"`: the exchange asks the panel's quantitative y to hold a categorical column, and one merged panel cannot hold both. Move the coord to the first member (whose coord IS the panel's), or give the member its own y with `resolve={"y": "independent"}`, which genuinely can hold it.

    Each refusal is scoped to the routes that actually cannot honor the declaration, which is not the same set for the two reasons: the panel-property reason refuses on all three merged-flat routes, while the channel-exchange reason refuses on the two that put every member on one shared axis pair (static `chart + chart`, `.interactive()`) and renders on `resolve={"y": "independent"}`, where the member's own y slot resolves from its own post-flip column. **A panel-scoped member coord still renders on the static shared-y route** too, where each member is its own composite leaf and nothing is dropped. The asymmetry is deliberate: refusing where a route can honor the declaration would regress a working composition, so it is the routes' real capabilities showing through rather than two answers to one question. Not addressed and flagged rather than half-built: interactive **zoom** over a mixed-coord panel, since a polar layer's wedges are baked polar pixels and WASM zoom would treat them as Cartesian. Static output is exact.
- **`mark_style` carries a `value_axis` declaration (#172(2)).** Additive and skip-when-absent, reusing the enum GH #77 added for exactly this purpose rather than introducing a parallel one. `desugar_density` stamps it, so a producer-horizontal series is a stated fact instead of something the renderer guesses from traced geometry. The heuristic it replaces (`traced_edge_axis`, which inferred horizontality from vertex order and misclassified a vertical area whose rows arrive unsorted) is deleted.
- **`ScaleSpec::Linear` carries a `systemInjected` provenance bit (#247A).** Additive, skip-when-false, so every pre-existing payload's bytes are unchanged. It distinguishes the zero-anchoring scale ferrum injects on a bar from one the caller wrote, which is the distinction the cross-mark domain union needs in order to know whose word to take. **Disclosed: the wire key is hand-settable.** The Python producer crosses the same boundary every user dict crosses, so `scale={"type": "linear", "systemInjected": True}` is accepted on a linear scale — a caller can assert ferrum's authorship of a scale the caller in fact wrote, and that leaf then yields to the union. Since the field's stated purpose is telling the two authorships apart, the advisory nature of that one spelling is disclosed rather than guarded; it is pinned by `tests/test_layer_union_provenance.py::test_hand_written_system_injected_scale_is_accepted_and_yields`. The `Chart.override(...)` registry deliberately **excludes** the leaf — there is no `.override(y_scale_systemInjected=True)` — with a test guard asserting the excluded key really is in the gate's accepted-key table, so the exclusion cannot rot into a coincidence.
- **A composite grid declares which cells share an extent (#182 follow-on).** `CompositeNode::Composite` gains `extent_alignments: Vec<{follower, leader, axis}>` (cell positions, additive, skip-when-empty), emitted by the `JointChart` and `ClusterMapChart` lowerings, so "this panel annotates that one" is declared rather than recognized from the grid's geometry. The shape predicate that had been guessing it is deleted: a clustermap is also a 2x2 grid with a hole, so the predicate separated two different figures by which corner was empty. A declaration naming a **nested composite** cell cannot be honored and now warns (`RenderWarning::ExtentAlignmentUnresolvable`) naming the follower, leader and axis, rather than dropping silently.
- **`stroke_dash=` accepts the named linetypes on every surface that accepts `stroke_dash=` (#124).** `mark_line(linetype="dashed")` worked and `mark_line(stroke_dash="dashed")` raised, because the gate read the caller's spelling rather than the canonical key, so the canonical form fell through to `float("dashed")`. Measured, the delta was exactly the five named linetypes — comma strings with or without spaces, float lists, int lists, tuples, both empty spellings and the junk refusal were already symmetric — so the canonical kwarg was a strict **subset** of its own alias, and it is now the superset. One normalization serves four surfaces: `MarkBase` mark kwargs, and `annotate_hline`/`annotate_vline`/`annotate_abline`, which take `stroke_dash=` and have **no** `linetype=` parameter of their own, so the named forms were unreachable there by any spelling. See Breaking for `fm.SelectionMark`, which went the other way. No value that was accepted before renders differently: 138 tests pin the form matrix across all four surfaces on the rendered `stroke-dasharray`, not on intermediate values.
- **`mark_area(position=Stack())` over a date or timestamp x renders (#184).** It raised for every input before. This is not a second keying model bolted on: a stack over a continuous quantitative axis already binned by exact value (an `Int32` `x:Q` stacks to its group sum, measured at ladder max `9.0` against an unstacked control of `6.0`), so exact-value keying was the established behavior and `Timestamp` was the one dtype the reader could not spell.
- `Bin(bins=)`, `mark_histogram(bins=)` and `displot(bins=)` accept a closed binning vocabulary (#193): `"sturges"`, `"fd"` / `"freedman_diaconis"`, a positive `int` (an exact bin count), a positive finite `float` (a bin **width**), or `None`. It is mutually exclusive with `bin_count`/`bin_width` — naming both refuses rather than dropping one answer. Everything outside the set refuses with a typed `ValueError` in the voice of the entry the caller invoked; see Breaking, below, for what this changes for callers who were relying on the old silence. `ferrum._core.validate_bin_rule(bins, ctx)` is the new shared replay hook that lets a figure entry refuse in its own name without a second copy of the vocabulary living in Python, mirroring the existing `validate_scale_dict`.
- `mark_histogram(position_by=)` (#194) names the column the `multiple=` position adjustment groups by, so forwarding the mode to the desugar does not cost the caller its grouping to the desugar's own position overwrite. It accepts a column name (`str`) or `None`; anything else refuses by name — `mark_histogram: position_by must be a column name (str) or None, not Color; received Color('g').`, with `Pass the column name itself: position_by='g'.` appended when a channel object was passed. It is deliberately documented as a column name rather than the figure layer's "str or encoding" spelling: `position_by` feeds `Stack(by=)`/`Dodge(by=)`, whose contract is `Optional[str]`, and unwrapping would silently widen a contract this change is only now creating.
- **`layer_kwargs={"<layer>": {...}}` aims mark kwargs at one sub-layer of a composite mark** (#199). A composite mark's own `**mark_kwargs` reach every layer it emits, so a bare `stroke=` on `mark_smooth` repaints the CI band as well as the fit line; `layer_kwargs=` is the narrower spelling. It is keyed on the same sub-layer vocabulary (`LAYER_NAME_CATALOG`) that the figure-level `mark={...}` override already uses, and it is owned by the pending dispatcher rather than by any one mark — so it is live on **every** composite routed through it, not only `mark_smooth`: `mark_boxplot(layer_kwargs={"median": {...}})`, `mark_violin`, `mark_pr` and the diagnostic family all take it. An unknown sub-layer name refuses by name — measured, `mark_smooth(layer_kwargs={"lines": ...})` gives `mark_smooth(layer_kwargs=): unknown sub-layer 'lines'; mark_smooth draws ['line', 'metrics', 'ribbon']. Pass style that should reach every layer as a plain mark kwarg instead, e.g. mark_smooth(opacity=0.5).`, and a mark with no named sub-layers says so: `mark_histogram(layer_kwargs=): unknown sub-layer 'bar'; mark_histogram draws no named sub-layers, so it has nothing to aim at.` `mark_smooth` declares the parameter explicitly and validates eagerly, so a typo refuses at the `mark_smooth(...)` call; every other composite takes it through `**mark_kwargs` and refuses at render — one rule and one message, two timings. When both spellings name the same key the narrower one wins — see Breaking, below. `lmplot(line_kws=...)` is unchanged and now forwards as `layer_kwargs={"line": ...}`.
- `CalibrationVisualizer(model, compare={"rival": other})` now works, with the same distinguished-base semantics as `ROCVisualizer`/`PRVisualizer`/`DiscriminationThresholdVisualizer` (#212): the positional model is the base (labelled `"base"`), `has_score` is `True`, `.score()` scores that model, and `_metrics` carries a single `calibration_error` — the base model's own, never pooled across rivals. The call previously raised `TypeError: CalibrationVisualizer.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'compare'`. The variadic `*models` overlay is unchanged (symmetric: `has_score=False`, `score()` returns `0.0`, one `calibration_error[<model name>]` key per model), and the two are mutually exclusive — several positional models together with `compare=` raises `ValueError` naming both idioms. `fit` resolves `compare=` through the same helper the three curve visualizers use, so `compare={"base": ...}` and a non-dict `compare=` refuse there with identical messages, and the rendered chart is byte-identical to `calibration_chart(model, X, y, compare=...)`. No existing call's output moved and no golden changed.
- A Rust `TransformWarning` channel (#162) now names every transform-level degrade that used to happen in silence — the all-NaN-panel family (`Smooth`, `Robust`, `RegressionData`, `LoessData`, `Kde`), `stat_smooth`'s `gam` `k` clamp, `LetterValue`'s named-depth cap, and `Impute`'s fallback shapes each surface a `UserWarning` naming the transform, the group (when grouped), and the reason. Render output is unchanged; only the diagnosis is new.
- A null groupby key now warns instead of silently pooling into an unlabeled group, across the whole partitioning family (#179/#197) — see Breaking, below, for the accompanying ordering and null-handling changes.
- `mark_raster()` validates its `aggregate`/`field` combination eagerly, at call time (#136), instead of deferring an invalid `aggregate` to a confusing render-time Rust error, or a missing `field=` to whenever `.encode()` happened to resolve.
- `_Facet.ncols`/`.nrows` (`Chart.facet(ncols=)`/`nrows=` and `encode(facet=fm.Facet(columns=))`) now validate as positive integers at construction (#238) — a non-positive or non-integer wrap count used to construct silently and either render nonsense or get silently overridden by inference.
- `BreakAxis(gap=...)` validates at construction (non-finite, malformed-shape, and reversed pairs all refuse) AND at the Rust wire gate for a hand-built `chart_config` dict (#92); the `annotations[]`/`structural[]` array-item key gate closes the one remaining carve-out in the chart-config wire validation (#148) — an unknown key inside an annotation or structural item now refuses by name instead of silently vanishing or slipping through.
- An ordinal-domain scale that resolves to a sub-pixel bandwidth now warns (`GroupCardinalityDegenerate`, #183), and a mark builder that discovers it has nothing to draw now names why instead of rendering a silent blank panel (`MarkBuildEmpty`, #118) — at five sites: `mark_segment` missing x2/y2, `mark_image` with no source, `mark_geoshape` with no geometry column at all, `mark_rect`'s box body on a continuous axis, and `mark_image` under `CoordPolar`/`CoordGeo`. Legitimate empties (a zero-row input, a documented per-row skip, a facet panel empty after a filter) still render silently, by design.
- The in-gap annotation-suppression warning now rolls up per axis instead of firing once per suppressed primitive (#240-3) — 4 `annotate_hline()` calls (8 primitives) now warn once, not eight times.
- A figure-level composite whose leaves structurally clone the same config mistake (e.g. `Repeat`/facet applying one `ChartConfig` override to every panel) now warns once instead of once per leaf (#140); a leaf's own data-condition warnings (a degrading transform, cardinality, an empty mark build) still fan out per leaf unchanged.
- `catplot(hue=fm.Color(...), hue_order=[...])` now composes instead of raising `TypeError` on every kind (#190) — `hue_order=` wins over a channel's own `sort=`, matching the `order=` precedent; a channel object's own `sort=` survives when no `hue_order=` is given.
- An unknown `cmap=`/`scheme=` name now refuses at Python desugar (`mark_raster`, `mark_hex`, `mark_contour`, `heatmap`, `clustermap`, `RenderConfig`) and warns at the Rust continuous-scheme resolve site instead of silently falling back to Viridis with no diagnostic (#240-4a/4b).
- `BreakAxis(gap=...)` bounds now accept temporal values — `str` (ISO-8601 date/datetime), `datetime.date`, `datetime.datetime`, `numpy.datetime64`, or `pandas.Timestamp` — coerced to epoch-milliseconds UTC at construction through the same conversion the encoding layer's TimeScale-domain coercion already uses (#230). A gap pairing a numeric bound with a temporal one, or carrying an unparseable string, refuses at construction naming the value; a temporal bound that lands outside a non-temporal axis's domain warns `BreakAxisIneffective` at render time rather than refusing. The break-axes guide's temporal example, previously documented as unsupported, now renders.
- `numpy.datetime64` is now accepted wherever ferrum accepts a temporal value in Python: a `scale={"type": "time", "domain": [...]}` domain element and an annotation coordinate (`ann.text(x=np.datetime64(...))`, every `annotate_*`/`ann.*` coordinate) join `BreakAxis(gap=...)`, which accepted it already. The `datetime64` conversion moved out of `BreakAxis`'s own module into `ferrum.annotation.coords.temporal_coord_to_epoch_ms`, the one canonical Python-side temporal seam all three go through, so the claim that they apply "the same conversion" is now literally true rather than true for three of four types. A `datetime64` domain element previously raised `TypeError`, and a `datetime64` annotation coordinate reached `json.dumps` and died with "Object of type datetime64 is not JSON serializable". One surface is deliberately not widened by this change and stays narrower: the Rust-backed `ferrum.TimeScale(domain=[...])` constructor extracts its domain in Rust and still refuses `numpy.datetime64` with its own accepted-forms `TypeError`. Spell that domain as a raw `scale={"type": "time", "domain": [...]}` dict, or convert to `pandas.Timestamp`/`datetime`, until the Rust extractor is widened to match.
- `BreakAxis(gap=...)` bounds now accept numpy numeric scalars (`np.int64`, `np.int32`, `np.float32`, ... — what `df["v"].max()` hands back) alongside `int`/`float`. Only `np.float64` worked before, and only because it subclasses `float`; the encoding layer's domain coercion has always accepted all of them. `bool` and `np.bool_` stay refused: a boolean is not a break region.
- **`Theme(cull_labels=)` separates "may labels be dropped" from "how much gap" (wave 6A, #146).** `cull_threshold` used to answer both with one integer, so `0` meant both "no gap" and "never cull", and "keep an 8 px gap but never drop a label" was unspellable. The new boolean key carries the permission alone: `Theme(cull_threshold=30, cull_labels=False)` holds every presentation stage to a 30 px gap and then keeps every label, landing flat or rotated depending on density. Every existing spelling keeps its meaning (`cull_threshold=0` is still gap 0 and never cull; a positive value still permits culling), and the default is unchanged (gap 8, culling permitted). See `ferrum-spec.md` §3.13's 2026-09-20 note.
- **`ferrum._core.mark_kwargs_fields()` publishes every `mark_style` key (wave 6B close).** It returns `MarkKwargsSpec`'s wire keys in declaration order, read from the struct's own serde field list, so Python code and tests can enumerate them without parsing Rust source.

### Fixed

- `.override(<channel>_scale_*=...)` reaches every layer (#159(10)). On `mark_boxplot`, `mark_violin`, `mark_errorbar`, `mark_boxen` and the catplot box/violin kinds, a scale override such as `.override(y_scale_domain=[0, 100])` was silently dropped because each layer carried its own copy of the channel's scale; it is now merged into every layer that declares the channel, including a channel only a layer declares, so every composition renders it exactly as the channel's own `scale=` would. Flat charts are byte-identical, except an unlayered mark that pairs `y` with `y2` (or `x` with `x2`) under a caller-written scale stating no `domain`, whose axis now spans the paired column too: `mark_bar().encode(x="g", y=fm.Y("v", scale={"reverse": True}), y2="w")` with `v = [1, 2]`, `w = [30, 40]` resolved its y domain from `v` alone, `(2, 1)`, leaving every `y2` endpoint off the axis, and now resolves `(40, 1)`. A refused override refuses with the scale gate's text on every path; on a channel only a layer declares that refusal is new (the override used to be accepted and ignored there). On a layered chart, every layer whose encoding carries an equal copy of a caller-written scale that states no `domain` now spans the axis together, plus their `y2`, and a sibling that declares no scale at all on the channel resolves on that axis and joins the same way, whichever layer carries the scale: the first caller-written scale in layer order establishes the axis, on `fm.layer` and `+` alike; a sibling that carries a genuinely different scale, or one that states a `domain`, stays out (before, such a scale read one bound column only). Under a caller scale that states a `domain`, a scale-less sibling now resolves on that stated axis on `fm.layer` too, as `+` already did: `fm.layer(mark_bar().encode(y=fm.Y("v", scale={"domain": [0, 100]})), mark_point().encode(y="w"))` with `w = [40, 60, 80]` drew the points over their own extent across the full panel height and now draws them at 40, 60 and 80 on the 0 to 100 axis. On `+`, when the first layer declares no scale, the axis follows the first layer that wrote one instead of pooling every later scale: `Chart(...).encode(y="s") + Chart(...).encode(y=fm.Y("c", scale={"padding": 0.2})) + Chart(...).encode(y=fm.Y("d", scale={"padding": 0.6}))` with `s = [10, 20, 30]`, `c = [1, 2, 3]`, `d = [50, 60, 70]` drew y ticks 5 to 70 and now draws 2 to 30, the same as `fm.layer`. A layer whose column cannot sit on the continuous axis a caller scale establishes now refuses on `+` in every such shape, including under a scale that states a `domain` and for a string layer that carries its own categorical scale (`mark_point().encode(y=fm.Y("v", scale={"domain": [0, 10]})) + mark_point().encode(y="label")` with a string `label` rendered with the second layer's marks silently absent, and now refuses with the shared-axis text `fm.layer` already used). Two route differences remain and are pinned as follow-ups rather than changed: a layer that stays out of the establishing scale draws on its own unlabelled scale on `fm.layer` but is clipped away, without a warning, on `+`; and the bar's injected zero anchor reaches the chart-level scale copy only, so a `reverse` bar scale beside a scale-less point spans 0 to 80 on `fm.layer` and 10 to 80 on `+`. This reaches composite marks with their own `scale=` (`mark_boxplot`, `mark_errorbar`, `mark_boxen`, `catplot(kind="box")` now cover their data), `regplot`/`lmplot` with a y `scale=` (now propagated to their band, line and metrics layers), layered charts built with `+` or `Chart.layer` whose layers carry equal caller scales or no scale at all, and `.override(...)`, which copies the scale onto every layer. Example: `mark_point().encode(y=fm.Y("v", scale={"reverse": True})) + mark_line().encode(y=fm.Y("w", scale={"reverse": True}))` with `v = [1, 2, 3]`, `w = [30, 40, 50]` drew y ticks 1 to 3 and now draws 5 to 50. A domain-less caller scale over an all-null column, flat or layered, resolved an infinite domain for a float column, or integer seeds of about ±9.2e18 for an integer column. It now resolves the `(0, 1)` fallback the auto path already used.
- **Breaking:** `Layer(transforms=...)` is refused instead of silently ignored (#176(8)). The list was accepted and serialized onto the layer, and no renderer ever read it, so `Layer(transforms=[fm.transform_filter(...)])` drew every row. A non-empty value now raises `ValueError` at construction reading `"Layer(transforms=[...]): a layer does not run its own transforms. Give the layer its own chart with .transform(...) and compose it with +, which routes the transform to that layer alone."`, and a hand-built `ChartSpec` carrying a non-empty `layers[i].transforms` raises the render-side form of the same text at render, naming layer `i`. An empty list or omission is unchanged. To transform one layer's rows, give that layer its own chart with `.transform(...)` and compose it with `+`.
- **Breaking:** the type you declared is the type that bands (#171). A `mark_boxplot`, `mark_violin`, `mark_errorbar` or `mark_boxen` whose category column carried an integer, unsigned, float or boolean dtype resolved to a **quantitative** scale, so its tick layer landed in the quantitative fallthrough and drew 8px stubs where band geometry belonged — measured on a 3-category `Int64` column, three box rects dropped entirely and the axis grew from 3 category ticks to 9 quantitative ones. Writing `x="cyl:N"` did not rescue it, and neither did `catplot`'s documented force-nominal default, because the declaration never survived long enough to be read: the composite desugar reduced both positional channels to bare field-name strings before any desugar ran, and six desugar sites then rebuilt the categorical channel untyped. The declared type now travels through the desugar, and where the caller declares nothing the five category-axis composite marks default that channel nominal. `mark_errorband` stays untyped, because its `x` is a genuine value axis, and `mark_tick` is deliberately untouched — it is a primitive, not a composite desugar, so it bands when its channel is declared nominal at any dtype and stubs as a true rug when the channel is an undeclared numeric. `catplot(native_scale=True)` is the negative control and stays quantitative: a blanket force-nominal implementation fails it.
- **Breaking:** `stat_letter_value` takes the group dtypes its siblings already took (#185). The issue reported that it rejected `Utf8` group columns; it was inverted. `Utf8` and `Categorical` were the only dtypes it accepted, and it refused every integer, unsigned, float and boolean that every sibling transform takes, so `mark_boxen` on an `Int64` category column raised outright where boxplot, violin and errorbar all rendered. `letter_value` was the last transform still carrying its own private group-key representation and its own hand-rolled dtype gate, years after the shared `group_key/` toolkit was created so that int/uint/bool support "exists once and cannot drift". Both private copies are retired rather than supplemented, routing through the same validator and partitioner `box_stats` uses, and the group column comes back in its **original dtype** rather than stringified. Routing through the shared partitioner surfaced a second defect: groups whose values are entirely null survived into the sort, where a `NaN` aggregate degrades the comparator to `Equal` against everything — measured, a `sort=median, Desc` request came back ascending. Empty groups are now skipped. `mark_boxen` on a numeric category column needed **both** this and #171: #171 alone left it raising, #185 alone left it drawing stubs with no boxes. Archaeology row NS-2 closes with it.
- **Breaking:** one answer to which dtypes a position adjustment can handle (#184). Three adjustments in one module held three opinions about which columns they could read, so the same frame behaved differently depending on which adjustment it met. On the **value** column, `Dodge` and `Jitter` gated on the Arrow dtype rather than on the resolved scale kind, so a `Float32`, `UInt8`, `UInt16`, `UInt32` or `Timestamp` measure was refused where a `Float64` was accepted; all three now retype through one helper and the eleven dtypes the crate's numeric reader accepts agree across `Stack`, `Dodge` and `Jitter`, asserted by a test that loops the matrix through all three rather than sampling one cell. On the **category** column, `Stack` carried a second, separate gate taking only `Float64`/`Utf8`, so `mark_bar(position=Stack()).encode(x="<Int64 column>:N", y=..., color=...)` refused while the identical column dodged, jittered and banded fine. That bespoke chain is deleted; the category column now keys through the same ordinal-category reader the band ladder is enumerated with, falling back to the temporal epoch reader, so two rows share a stack bin if and only if they share a **band** wherever a band ladder exists. Neither hole is a regression introduced by #171: the refusal was measured byte-identical with and without it, by reverting the desugar and re-probing. A temporal column declared **ordinal** still refuses, from a different site (the band ladder cannot enumerate `Timestamp`); that refusal is pinned rather than deleted, and filed separately.
- **Breaking:** `nice=True` on a typed scale class does what the same key does in a raw dict (#153). `LinearScale(nice=True)`, `TimeScale(nice=True)` and their `LogScale`/`SymlogScale` siblings accepted the kwarg and always serialized `nice: false`, so a chart written with the typed class rendered un-niced ticks while the identical chart written as `{"type": "linear", "nice": true}` rendered niced ones. Measured on the same data: ticks `1..9` against `0..10`, and a first time tick of `2024-01-04` against `2023-12-28`. The gap was exactly the **data-derived** domain, where nicing is deferred to render and the flag had nowhere to live — the structs had no `nice` field, so the value was discarded at construction and the wire conversion hardcoded `false`. All four classes now retain and emit it; no new wire key was needed, since `nice` already exists on `ScaleSpec` and only the class path had to reach it. The explicit-domain case was never broken (those classes nice eagerly at construction) and stays byte-stable. `TimeScale`'s own docstring has promised this behavior all along and was simply false; it is left untouched because the fix makes it true.
- **Breaking:** the sort you wrote is the sort that applies (#95). `sort="-y"` ordered a bar chart's categories correctly until `.coord(fm.CoordFlip())` was added, at which point it aggregated the **category** column, warned twice, and fell back to insertion order — and the spelling that worked under flip was `sort="-x"`, for a value the caller had put on `y`, which nobody would write. The channel token in a sort shorthand is the caller's own text in authored space, while the fields it was matched against were built from the post-flip rendering encoding; the two sides of one lookup lived in different coordinate spaces. It now resolves in authored space through the same involution the crate already established for user-facing channel tokens, rather than a second flip mapping — the crate has exactly one flip expression, and #113 reinforced that rule three commits earlier. The `sort=fm.SortField(...)` object form names a field rather than a channel and was therefore already flip-invariant, so the two forms agreed before the flip and disagreed after it; they now agree throughout, which is what the shorthand being sugar for the object form is supposed to mean. The same two-spaces defect sat in the `shape`, `stroke_dash` and `color` categorical domains, which build their sort the same way, and is fixed with it. Also closed: an **ignored** sort spec was reported once by the provisional placeholder pass and again by the panel-real pass, and now warns once — stripped only after a panel has actually resolved, not by the cheaper placeholder filter, which was measured to drop the warning to zero for a chart whose data is empty after transforms.
- **Breaking:** a flipped chart's marks and its axis agree again (#113). Under `CoordFlip`, scene construction merged the **authored** chart-level encoding with the post-flip layer-0 encoding, and since the overlay only overwrites channels the overlay itself has, a channel the flip had already moved (`y2` → `x2`) left the overlay's `y2` empty and the pre-flip `y2` survived alongside the post-flip `y`, widening the wrong axis. Measured on a flipped `mark_bar` with a chart-level `y2`: the marks were placed on a y scale spanning roughly `0..72.9` while the axis ticks in the same SVG read `0..3` — a 23-fold divergence inside one file. The chart preparation path never had this bug, because it builds its rendering spec by replacing the encoding wholesale, which is why the ticks were right and only the marks were wrong; the two sites disagreeing was the defect. The base encoding is now flipped before it is overlaid, the convention the domain union and the layer fallback already follow, so no second flip mapping enters the crate. The overlay itself stays, because a layer-0 with its own `data_source` inherits only non-positional channels and the overlay is what recovers its `x`/`y`. Guarded on the flip, so every unflipped chart is byte-identical.
- **Breaking:** a series closes its fill on its own baseline, and a flipped ribbon draws (#172(2), #172(3)). Two mark drawers hard-coded a y-axis idiom that `CoordFlip` invalidates. A `CoordFlip`'d `mark_area` traced its vertices correctly and then closed the filled region on the panel **bottom** — byte-for-byte the closure an unflipped chart emits — so the filled region came out very nearly the complement of the correct one; the same hard-code is reached with **no flip at all** by `mark_density(orientation="horizontal")`, whose near-zero tails degenerate the spurious region to a sliver, which is how the defect came to sit latent inside a blessed golden. Separately, a `CoordFlip`'d `mark_ribbon` rendered **nothing**, silently: the flip had moved the authored `y2` into `x2` and the drawer read only `y2`, so `mark_errorband`, `mark_smooth(ci=)`, the learning and validation curves and the QQ CI region all went band-less with it. The band's curve axis is now a function of the channel actually read, and the value axis is declared rather than inferred. The three signals compose by **precedence**, not disjunction: a declaration is a statement about the rendered series and travels with the channels through the flip, so it answers outright, while `CoordFlip` and the stack stamp describe a transformation of a series that was vertical by default and speak only in its absence. As a disjunction, a producer-horizontal density under `CoordFlip` stayed "horizontal" on the flip's word alone and closed 2.9px of sliver at the left edge — the two flips must cancel. `mark_ribbon` joins the coord-flip test matrix, whose absence is why this survived.
- **Breaking:** a swatch shows the encoding it stands for (#111). When `stroke_dash` or `shape` shares its field with `color`, the aux legend block is suppressed so the reader sees one legend instead of two — and the surviving color legend then drew three identical **solid circles** over marks that were solid, `6,3` and `2,3`, or over marks that were circle, square and cross, making the mapping the legend exists to explain unreadable. The surviving entries now carry the suppressed channel's identity. The issue's own prescription (thread the dash pattern into the color entries) was measured to be a no-op, because the color swatch is a hard-coded circle and the pattern is only read inside the line arm, so the swatch **kind** had to change; shape needed only the thread, since the renderer already reads the shape name ahead of the symbol-kind match. Identities are matched to color entries **by label, never by index**, because both domains are built over the same field with independent sort specs and index pairing silently transposes under `sort="descending"` — a reordered `configure_color(domain=)` exposed the same class one layer down, where the resync had to clone a surviving category's entry rather than rebuild it from label and symbol, since the line symbol survives a rebuild while the pattern does not. Three adjacent behaviors settle with it: a suppressed channel's explicit `title=` is no longer silently discarded (and a genuine two-title disagreement warns); `color=fm.Color(..., legend=None)` with a same-field dash no longer erases **both** blocks, since with no color legend there is nothing to duplicate — that gate now asks whether the color legend **enumerates** the field rather than whether its scale is categorical, so a colorbar plus a categorical shape or dash renders the aux block too; and a chart binding `color`, `shape` **and** dash to one field shows the shape, because the glyphs are painted fill-only and a dash array on them paints nothing, with the losing pattern no longer written onto the entry at all and the drop warned.
- **Breaking:** a gutter is reserved only for chrome that is drawn (#182, closing #94). Layout reserved margins for chrome it never drew: hiding an axis kept the axis title's gutter, and a polar chart — which has no Cartesian axes to hide at all — reserved Cartesian margins regardless, so the plot area came out smaller than the drawn chrome justified and the chart sat off-centre in its own frame. Measured before the fix, `.axis(show=False)` reserved a 23.729px phantom title gutter per side plus a 14.046px phantom label band on the y side, and a polar chart reserved 23.214px left and 43.039px bottom. The gate now asks whether the axis actually **draws**, which is what the sibling label-band check three lines away already did, so `.axis(show=False)` on a 640x480 chart with 16px padding collapses to exactly `(16, 16, 608, 448)`, `fm.Axis(labels=False)` collapses the label band alone, and polar reserves nothing Cartesian. Scoping the gate to the `show` flags would have closed #182 and left #94 open, which is why it reads "is it drawn". `plots/matrix.py`'s clustermap dendrogram `title=""` workaround — which existed only to reach the title-width route so a hidden axis's phantom gutter stopped eating the dendrogram's share of `dendrogram_ratio` — is removed rather than left beside the rule that replaces it.
- **Breaking:** a composite's satellite panels align with the panel they annotate (#182 follow-on). Removing the phantom gutter exposed a real defect it had been masking: a `jointplot` marginal and a `clustermap` dendrogram are each their own leaf, laid out independently and fitted into a slot, so their plot rects and the centre panel's had no reason to agree — they *looked* aligned because the phantom inset them by roughly the centre's axis band. Measured post-#182, the `jointplot_kde_hist` top marginal spanned x `53.377..624.0` against the joint panel's `67.423..547.025`, and the `clustermap_basic` column dendrogram spanned `176.0..744.0` against the heatmap's `213.4..674.8`, 37px past it on the left and 69px on the right, its leaves landing between the columns and its last drop over the colorbar. It was caught by comparing the regenerated golden against its **pre-fix** rasterization rather than judging the new render alone, which read as correct in isolation. A panel that annotates another now spans exactly that panel's extent on the axis the two share and keeps its own on the other, imposed through the same seam the #89A overlay pre-pass uses, at the same single point in layout. Clustermap gets the fix too, rather than being excluded to avoid re-blessing goldens: worst leaf-over-column error drops 123px to 50px. Two details are load-bearing: the imposed extent is scaled by the follower's native size over the leader's, because the grid planner ratio-fits each cell, and chrome is now told apart from blank padding, which is what lets the row dendrogram (landing 2.67px inside its own 16px padding) align at all. **Named as pre-existing and not fixed here:** a dendrogram's own x scale places its leaves at the extremes of its plot rect (≈1.7% and 98.3%) while the heatmap places its columns at band centres (12.5% and 87.5%), so leaf-over-column is closer but not exact. That is the dendrogram chart's scale, not the panel's extent, and it is filed rather than papered over.
- **Breaking:** a scale ferrum wrote is not a scale the caller wrote (#247A). A bar chart's value scale is injected by ferrum itself to anchor the axis at zero, and that injected scale was indistinguishable from one the caller wrote — a caller-written scale rightly short-circuits the cross-mark domain union, so a bar overlaid with another mark bypassed the union and drew its bars against a domain its own axis did not describe. The injection now marks its authorship, and the resolver's auto-zero-anchor gate reaches layered specs through `spec.layers` rather than reading any present scale as the caller's word; a scale the caller wrote keeps the flag withheld, so it still wins. The zero-widening arithmetic's third hand-copy folds into the shared helper the new gate's anchor flows into, and a comment claiming to mirror a block that had itself become a call to that helper is deleted. See Breaking for exactly which four shapes move, measured over a 25-shape hash corpus.
- **Breaking:** a band exists only if something is drawn in it (#247B). A layered chart built from two DataFrames grew a `null` band on its ordinal axis that no mark ever occupied — the merge's diagonal concat pads each frame's columns over the other frame's rows, and those manufactured nulls reached categorical domain resolution as if the caller had supplied them, so every real band was narrower for it and the axis described a category the chart did not draw. The rule is now stated positively: a null in a positional ordinal column claims the null-category band only when some null-category row carries a non-null value on the **opposite positional channel**. Nullness only — a non-finite paired value (`NaN`/`±inf`) counts as occupancy, because the marks still resolve it to a pixel rather than skip the row. The standing policy that a genuine null category carrying a real value gets its own band is intact and pinned in both directions, so a null group and a real `0`/`false`/`""` group remain two categories (FA-9); null-free columns are byte-identical. The fix is **not** scoped to the merge, where the design doc first placed it: a single unmerged chart over `cat=["a","b","c",None], val=[1.0,2.0,3.0,None]` renders the same phantom band and draws three marks, so a merge-scoped fix was provably incomplete — and no merge-side data-shape change could be complete either, since a merged batch must be at least as tall as its taller frame, so with unequal heights the shorter frame's columns necessarily carry entries its data never supplied and all three candidate fill values are wrong. Two claims the briefs carried were measured false rather than implemented and are recorded so they are not repeated: the opposite positional channel is not the `x2`/`y2` **span** partner (wiring the rule there would have made the fix silently inert, since `x2` is `None` on essentially every chart), and the stack adjustment does not consume this enumeration at runtime, so the axis ladder and the stack ladder cannot drift here — the stale invariant comment claiming they were one partition is rewritten rather than left standing.
- **Breaking:** the shared ordinal axis covers every layer's categories (added mid-wave by user ruling). `Chart({cat: [a, b, c]}) + Chart({cat: [x, y, z]})` drew **three marks for six rows**. A layered chart's ordinal axis read its categories off the chart-level column alone and never unioned its layers', so the second layer's marks were dropped for want of a band and its categories never even reached the axis labels; the numeric domain has had a cross-layer union for exactly this since before the wave, so the invariant "the shared axis covers every layer's extent" was half implemented. Every category any layer's positional column carries now gets a band, ordered by **first appearance in layer order** — not a new semantic, but the existing within-layer first-appearance rule carried across the layer boundary rather than replaced by sorting. Ordering and scope were a user ruling, taken because the choice sets every band position and is visible in every render. #247B's null rule is preserved exactly, including its twelve tests, which are unedited; one sub-rule is new and deliberately narrower than a naive extension, in that per layer the null band is judged against the positional field that layer itself binds and a layer binding none drops the null category — carrying #247B's "no opposite channel bound → keep the band" arm across the layer boundary instead would keep a band for an injected reference-line column and draw four rule lines where one is correct. Deliberately unchanged: the **explicit-scale** ordinal path still does not union layers, because the explicit numeric path has the identical property and fixing one side alone would break that symmetry. Removing #247B's phantom band removed the only visible hint of this defect, which is why the two shipped together: the marks were already lost, but the after-state was a clean, plausible three-category chart silently holding half its data.
- **Breaking:** the canonical spelling stops being the poor relation of its own alias (#124). See Added for the widening, and Breaking for `fm.SelectionMark`'s new refusal and the decision this reverses. One divergence found on the way is closed with it: the annotation constructors' mark kwargs and their `AnnotationLine` primitive each derived their own dash representation from the raw parameter, so an overlay could disagree with the mark it annotates; both now derive from a single capture.
- **Breaking:** a channel's `title=` is now the title its legend prints (#90). `title=` on `Color`/`Size`/`Shape`/`StrokeDash` was honored on axes and discarded by every legend builder, which read the field name directly — so `fm.Color("species", type="N", title="Species")` produced a legend headed `species`. One resolver now serves the swatch legend, both colorbars, the conditional legend, the facet band and every auxiliary block, with legend-title suppression spelled the way axes already spell it. The field name is still the default when no `title=` is given.
- **Breaking:** an untyped `Color` on `hue=` is treated as a discriminator, not a gradient (#130). The figure-level helpers decided categorical-vs-continuous by asking whether the caller passed a bare string or a channel *object*, so `hue="species"` and `hue=fm.Color("species")` read the same column two different ways — the typed object fell through to a continuous color scale. The helper now asks whether the caller *stated* a type. `fm.Color("v", type="Q")` still gets its gradient.
- **Breaking:** `pr_chart(iso_lines=True)`'s reference curves render in the grey they declare (#121). The iso-line layer bound its grouping key `_iso_f` to the `color` channel, but the chart's color scale is keyed on `class`, so every `"F=0.2".."F=0.8"` lookup missed and the line mark's stroke fallback painted `paint.fill` — theme blue at the layer's `opacity=0.6` — instead of the literal `stroke="#AAAAAA"` the layer had set all along. The key now travels on `detail`, the channel that groups without painting, mirroring `desugar_parallel_coordinates`. The stale `_iso_f` entry in the color-binding allowlist, whose rationale asserted the curves were already grey and that the single-color rendering was an unrelated defect, is deleted with it — both clauses were false.
- **Breaking:** `catplot(kind="count")` accepts a channel object like its seven siblings (#192). `count` was the only kind that read the raw `x=`/`y=` argument again after the shared splitter had resolved it, so `catplot(..., kind="count", x=fm.X("g"))` died with a bare PyO3 `TypeError: argument 'field': 'X' object is not an instance of 'str'` — naming neither `catplot`, nor `kind="count"`, nor a working route — while `x : str or encoding` was already the documented contract. It now derives the aggregated field from the resolved channel, the same idiom the estimating kinds already use, which also closes a latent second defect on the same line; see Breaking, below. The bare-string path is byte-identical, SHA-256-pinned.
- **Breaking:** `displot(kind="ecdf", hue=...)` renders per-class curves (#200). The ecdf branch is the one `displot` path that builds its own `Bin`, and it built one without the group key, so the transform discarded the `hue=` column before the encoding referred to it. The branch now hands the `Bin` the group key, on a support the groups share. The no-hue ecdf path is byte-identical.
- **Breaking:** a layered chart is judged by the marks it draws, and an axis may bind from the layer that binds it (#201-D). Lowering a `(tick + line)` composition leaves `mark="tick"` at the spec root as a placeholder, and single-axis-exemption eligibility was decided from that root mark alone — so the resolver granted the exemption to a chart whose line layer genuinely needs both axes and synthesized a `0…1` dummy y scale. Measured on a rug layered under a density curve, the curve was squashed into the foot of the panel: polyline y extent `(368.316, 416.911)` of a 480px canvas under y tick labels `1, 0.9, … 0`. It now renders at `(24.0, 412.961)` under the density column's own units, `0.13, 0.12, … 0.01`. Eligibility is now decided by every layer that paints; binding takes the chart encoding when there is one, else the first layer eligible to supply it. A chart-level or layer-0 binding always wins, so the change is bounded to specs whose merged chart+layer-0 encoding leaves a positional channel unbound: specs that resolve through the generic two-axis path are untouched, while **specs that resolved through the single-axis exemption with an axis unbound do move, by design** — that is the defect being fixed, and the geometry above is an instance of it. The value axis of a bare `mark_tick + mark_line` layering is **deliberately left untitled**: the fallback resolves a scale, not the caller-written binding an axis title is a claim about, so titling from it would assert something no caller said. To get a title, write `.encode(y=fm.Y("density", title="Density"))` on the composition's own chart encoding, or on the **first** chart of the pair (layer 0) — a `title=` on any later layer is ignored, being exactly the fallback binding just described as not title-bearing. Measured on `tick + line`: `tick_titled + line_plain` and `line_titled + tick_plain` both render `Density`, while `tick_plain + line_titled` renders no title at all and `line_plain + tick_titled` renders layer 0's bare field name `d`. Watch the trap in the third of those — the density column is the *line's*, so titling the line chart is the obvious move, and in `tick + line` the line is the second layer. The accompanying new typed refusal, and the twelve pre-existing silent drops this closes, are under Breaking, below.
- **Breaking:** `displot(x=..., kind="rug", kde=True)` renders with a titled density axis (#201-B). This composition is one the docstring advertised twice and named rug for explicitly, and it *raised* — `encoding 'y' expected EncodingSpec, got None` — because the x-only root encoding fell through the generic x/y check. #201-D made it render with the correct extent and tick labels, but through the binding fallback, which resolves a scale rather than a chart-level binding, so the density axis came out untitled. `displot` now binds at chart level the density column the overlay already names, and the axis is titled `density` — the KDE transform's own output column name, exactly what `displot(kind="kde")` and `displot(kind="kde", rug=True)` already title the same axis and what `kind="ecdf"` does with `count`. The curve's extent and tick labels are unchanged from #201-D, the rug's 200 ticks still sit at the axis base, and `hue=` still yields two curves and split ticks, now titled. Not fixed here and pre-existing: `displot(y="v", kind="rug", kde=True)` silently drops the KDE overlay, because the overlay block is gated on the x variable, and renders a bare vertical rug.
- **Breaking:** the binning rule you name is the rule that bins, and the rule the wire reports (#193). `displot`'s `bins=` was handed to a door that read only an integer count, so every other spelling was silently discarded and the chart fell back to Sturges. Measured pre-fix: `bins="fd"`, `"freedman_diaconis"`, `"auto"`, `"scott"`, `"stone"`, `bins=0.5` and `bins=np.int64(9)` all rendered the identical 11-rect Sturges histogram with an empty transform payload; `bins=True` rendered a 1-bin histogram, because `bool` subclasses `int`; `bins=0` raised a `ValueError` naming `Bin` rather than `displot`; and `bins=-3` leaked a raw `OverflowError` out of the renderer. The door now hands the value to the parser its siblings already use. Serialization is fixed with it: `BinMode::FreedmanDiaconis` mapped to the same empty wire payload as Sturges, so `to_dict()`/`to_json()` reported Sturges for a chart that had drawn Freedman–Diaconis. FD now carries an explicitly-named additive `bin_rule` key while Sturges stays the wire default — the absence of all three mode keys — so every pre-existing payload keeps its exact bytes; a payload naming `bin_rule` *and* a numeric key refuses rather than dropping one answer. `repr(Bin("x", bins="fd"))` gains a trailing `, bins='freedman_diaconis'`; every other mode's repr is byte-identical. Six public-API behavior changes ship with this; all six are under Breaking, below.
- **Breaking:** `multiple="stack"` on `displot` produces a stack (#194). `displot` built its own position adjustment from `multiple=` and never forwarded the mode into `mark_histogram`, so the underlying `Bin` ran at its default and binned **each group over its own data range**. The groups' bars therefore shared no x position and were not even the same width — measured on two 300-point normals at means 0 and 3, bar widths of `31.34 px` and `34.19 px`, and **16 distinct x starts where a stack needs 8**. The stack adjustment had no pairs to pair, and the render was a side-by-side chart wearing a stack's name. What a `multiple=` mode means is now answered in one place, which decides the position adjustment *and* the binning extent together, because bars can only stack if every group binned against the same edges. `multiple="layer"`, `multiple="dodge"`, the no-hue `"stack"` path and the `kind="kde"` branch are byte-identical. A rider on the same hunk: `catplot`'s dodge read the raw `hue=` argument, so `hue=fm.Color("g")` died with an untyped `TypeError: Object of type Color is not JSON serializable`; it now reads the field name. Not fixed and disclosed: `displot(kind="kde", multiple="stack")` still does not forward the mode to `mark_density`.
- **Breaking:** a fit that draws alone knows whose columns it drew, and `line_kws` reaches the line (#199). The fit-family builder bound every layer to the transform's own output columns with no `title=`, and the renderer resolves an axis title from layer 0 only — so whenever no scatter happened to sit at layer 0, the value axis was titled `ci_lower`, the confidence band's **lower bound**, the wrong quantity rather than merely an internal spelling, and the predictor axis was titled `x`. Measured over the full 40-cell `method × ci × show_metrics` matrix under `scatter=False`: **7 of 40 cells** were correct before (exactly the seven `mark_smooth`-routed methods at `ci=None, show_metrics=False`, where a single-line shortcut keeps the chart-level encoding), **40 of 40** after. Separately, `line_kws` was applied by rewriting the finished chart's layers, which on the smooth-routed branch do not exist yet — the chart carries a pending stat mark until render — so the guard was falsy and the styling vanished: **12 of 40** cells applied it before, **40 of 40** after, the 28 dropped cells being exactly the seven smooth-routed methods × 4. Every branch now forwards it at construction. One golden moved, `residplot_lowess.svg`, whose y-axis title changes and nothing else. Two user-visible consequences are under Breaking, below.
- **Breaking:** `displot(multiple="dodge")` bins every group on shared edges. `"dodge"` joins `"stack"` and `"fill"` in requiring one binning extent, leaving `"layer"` as the only mode that may bin per group — it is the only one that asks nothing of its neighbours. The band a dodged bar takes its slot in *is* the bin, so the bins have to be the same bins for every group before a slot means anything. Previously each hue group binned over its own range: measured on a two-group frame, 9 bars of one width against 10 of another, sharing **zero** x positions. See Breaking, below, for the measured after-state.
- **Breaking:** a dodged span mark subdivides its band instead of overrunning it, and stops dropping a bar. `apply_dodge`'s continuous branch offset only the band column and left the span's other end alone, so each group's rect came out `bin_width ∓ offset` wide rather than a share of the bin — and a negative offset could push the leftmost bin start below the x-domain, where the scale returns `NaN`, the pixel fails to resolve and the row is skipped entirely, so the bar was **missing**, not merely mis-sized. Both endpoints now move together into the group's own sub-slot. This reaches every dodged span mark, not only `displot` — see Breaking, below.
- An all-zero-extent bar batch now says why it drew nothing instead of rendering a silent blank panel. The zero-extent guard that stops an invisible rect reaching the SVG (see T10b, below) left nothing downstream able to tell that silence from a chart that legitimately had nothing to draw; the four rect builders now set the batch's empty-reason when — and only when — the guard emptied a batch that had rows to draw. Measured on `importance_chart` over an all-zero importance column: `bar layer 0 drew nothing: mark_bar() computed a zero width or height for all 4 of its rects (every value equal to its own baseline, or an empty group in every bin); a zero-extent fill paints nothing, so none were emitted`. A batch already empty for a documented reason (zero rows, null categories, non-finite values, unresolvable pixels) never reaches the guard and stays quiet, and the non-zero control emits nothing. **SVG bytes are unchanged everywhere** — but it is a new `UserWarning`, so see Breaking, below.
- `displot` refuses in its own voice, and stops dropping a mark it was asked for (close remediation). One helper now answers for `hist`/`kde`/`ecdf` and both overlays, so `mark_histogram() requires .encode(x=...)` and its `mark_density()` twin no longer escape from a `displot` call: `displot(kind='hist', y='v') is not supported -- kind='hist' draws the distribution of the variable x= names and computes the other axis itself (a count, a density, a cumulative frequency), so a y= variable has nowhere to go. Pass x='v', or kind='rug', which draws its ticks against either axis.` `kind="rug"` is deliberately excluded, since a tick draws against either axis. `displot(bins=)` now warns when the rule is inert for `kind="kde"`/`"rug"`, and only when the caller actually stated a rule — `bins=None` and `bins="sturges"` both mean the default a non-binning kind would have used anyway. See Breaking for the `kind="rug", y=..., kde=True` case, which now refuses.
- `lmplot` and `residplot` accept an encoding object for `x=`/`y=` on **every** method (close remediation). Seven of `lmplot`'s ten methods already did, because they route through `.encode()`; the three that construct a transform directly, plus `residplot`, leaked a binding-layer error instead — measured, the raw PyO3 `TypeError: argument 'x': 'X' object is not an instance of 'str'` from `logistic`/`glm`/`robust`, and polars' `TypeError: cannot turn 'ferrum.encoding.positional.X' into selector` from `residplot`. Accepting rather than refusing, because refusing would have regressed seven working paths. The channel still reaches `.encode()`, so its `title=` survives: measured, `lmplot(x=fm.X("bill", title="Total bill (USD)"), method="robust")` renders that title rather than the bare column name.
- `mark_tick` joins the mark×dtype color-parity sweep it was missing from (#131), with a Python twin pinning four ticks per swatch in both the string and categorical dtypes. No behavior change — this is the coverage that makes the color read batch E gave `mark_tick` unable to regress silently.
- **Breaking:** eleven grouped transforms (`Aggregate`, `Summary`, `BoxStats`, `ErrorExtent`, `Violin`, `Pivot`, `DataAggregate`, `DensityData`, and — with a warning, not a row-drop — `Impute`/`Smooth`/`Robust`/`DataStack`/`JoinAggregate`/`DataWindow`) move onto one shared partitioner (`group_key::group_partition_multi`), closing the repeated-groupby-name gap everywhere at once and installing one null-key policy instead of eighteen hand-rolled ones. See Breaking, below, for the two disclosed output changes this migration carries.
- `transform_density(field, groupby=[...])` now honors every groupby dimension — it previously grouped by only the first and silently discarded the rest.
- `transform_pivot`/`transform_aggregate`/`transform_density` now refuse a repeated `groupby` column name at Python construction, matching every typed transform constructor's existing behavior.
- A one-element (or otherwise not-length-2) explicit `domain=`/`range=` on any of the seven continuous scale kinds now refuses with a typed message instead of panicking with an out-of-bounds index (#152).
- Six color-range parse sites (three encoding-level, three chart-config-level, including a previously-unwarned continuous `filter_map` sibling) now raise instead of silently discarding the entire explicit range in favor of the theme palette (#126). `ferrum.color.to_hex` now keeps a tuple's 4th (alpha) component on every accepted form instead of silently dropping it, and raises a typed error for a non-numeric component instead of an escaped bare `TypeError` (#122). Chart-config color fields (`AxisConfig`, `LegendConfig`, `TitleConfig`, `GridAxisConfig`, `GridConfig`) now refuse an unparseable color string at construction instead of silently keeping it unresolved (#107). A time-typed format on a non-temporal resolved scale now refuses at all three surfaces that accept a format (per-channel, chart-level, and the facet independent-axis path) instead of rendering a nonsense epoch-date artifact (#133).
- `mark_text`/`mark_label`/`mark_rect` (heatmap cell annotations) now propagate a typed error for an unreadable `text=` column instead of silently rendering a blank/degraded panel (#166).
- `Chart.__add__` (`a + b`) now preserves each layer's own `data_source` instead of unconditionally overwriting it — a multi-output composite mark (`mark_qq`, `mark_violin`, `mark_boxplot`, …) now composes via `+` in either order; two different composite charts that happen to share a transform's auto-generated name now refuse at composition time, naming the layer and the colliding output, instead of silently letting one side's data overwrite the other's at render (#198).
- `rank1d(algorithm="covariance")` now refuses a `y` whose length doesn't match `X`'s row count with a typed message instead of panicking, and drops null-`y` rows (with a warning naming the count) instead of silently zero-imputing them, corrupting every feature's score (#204). `rank1d`/`rank2d` now refuse an X-less fitted estimator on every algorithm, not only `algorithm="covariance"` (#216).
- **Breaking:** a multi-chunk `pyarrow.Table` passed to `rank1d_chart`/`rank2d_chart`/`Rank1DVisualizer`/`Rank2DVisualizer` is now consumed in full instead of silently truncated to its first chunk (N1). The raw-data coercer took `X.to_batches()[0]`, so any Table built by `pa.concat_tables`, a multi-file `parquet.read_table`, or a `RecordBatchReader` drain (the ordinary provenance of a real chunked Table) computed every rank statistic over a fraction of its rows — measured on a 200-row fixture split into two 100-row chunks, `rank1d_chart(chunked, algorithm="variance")`'s top score for column `b` read **1.169347** (the first 100-row chunk only) against the correct **1.018316** (all 200 rows), matching the `ModelSource` path and a single-chunk Table of the same data. The covariance path did not silently compute a wrong number; it refused with a misleading `"y length must match X row count"`, since X's row count was silently halved while `y` kept its full length — it now renders and matches the `ModelSource` path. An empty 2-column `pyarrow.Table` also used to return zero features (a smaller instance of the same coercer bug discarding the schema along with the rows); it now returns 2 features scored 0.0, matching an empty `polars.DataFrame` with the same schema.
- Nine precomputed-diagnostic methods (`roc_curve`, `pr_curve`, `cumulative_gain`/`lift_curve`, `calibration_curve`, `confusion_matrix`, `classification_report`, `predictions`, `discrimination_threshold`) now raise a typed error naming the calling figure function and the observed-vs-required shape for a malformed `y_pred`, instead of leaking `IndexError`/`ArrowInvalid`/an unhashable-type `TypeError`/a numpy broadcast error, or silently computing a wrong-but-plausible result for a 1-D `y_pred` against a genuinely multiclass target (#208).
- `pr_chart(iso_lines=True)` (and any caller reaching the shared metric-label/direct-label enumerators with a null-carrying group column) no longer emits polars' internal "Comparisons with None always result in null" warning (#220).
- Per-mark honored-kwarg vocabularies (#243): a mark kwarg not honored by that specific mark now refuses by name at construction instead of being silently accepted and dropped at the Rust wire — e.g. `mark_bar(arrow=True)` (a segment-only field) now raises, naming `mark_bar`'s real honored set. Settles #154 (`mark_bar(band_size=...)` — `band_size` is honored only by `mark_tick`/`mark_rect`). The refusal timing differs by mark family: a primitive mark (`mark_bar`, `mark_point`, …) refuses immediately at the `mark_*()` call, while a composite/statistical mark (`mark_boxplot`, `mark_violin`, …) constructs successfully and refuses only once the pending mark resolves, at `.to_dict()`/`.to_svg()` — an existing property of the pending-desugar architecture that this change now surfaces for the first time.
- `ferrum._warn.warn_once`'s dedupe identity widens from `(channel, kwarg)` to `(channel, kwarg, message)` (#240) — two calls on the same pair with genuinely different messages now both emit, once each, instead of the second being silently swallowed. Two carve-outs that said "planned for a future Phase" (a permanently untrue statement for a permanent refusal) now state the true, permanent fact.
- **Breaking:** `BreakAxis`'s piecewise scale is now built against the broken scale's own padded pixel range instead of the raw plot rect, and a tick label under a break now translates rigidly on its own tick's anchor instead of having its own drawn baseline-offset coordinate reverse-mapped (#226).
