Visibility widens only to `pub(super)` inside each module's own tree; nothing became `pub` or `pub(crate)`, and no field widened.
- **Untyped state becomes types (wave 6A).** Paint provenance becomes `PaintSource`/`ClearablePaint` in place of four parallel booleans and tuple returns (#110 part 1, byte-stable over a 64-shape corpus). The break axis becomes the shared two-variant `AlignAxis`, the enum `ExtentAlignment` already used (#229(2)). `AxisStyleOverrides`' 34 flat fields partition into six clusters, with `Claimed<T>` replacing hand-rolled claimed-booleans and one `LabelBandCtx` shared by the two x-label layout functions (#145). The six format predicates become `FormatKind::resolve` (#147), and `cull_threshold`'s two meanings become `LabelGapPolicy { gap_px, cull }` (#146).
- **The unreachable per-channel `condition` wire route is deleted (wave 6A, #237).** Every public spelling already routes through `ChartSpec.conditionals`. The Rust `EncodingSpec.condition` field is kept, because the struct denies unknown fields and dropping it would turn a previously valid saved spec into a parse error.
- **The facet-extent pin is guarded by behavior, not a restated list (wave 6A, #196).** `fix_transform_extents_for_facet` keeps its wildcard arm; a test now drives every extent-carrying transform kind through the real function and fails by name if a new kind misses the pin.
- **`#229`(4), splitting break_axis's measure-and-mutate walker into a read-only visit plus a mutating one, is closed WONTFIX-by-design (user ruling).** The walker's own documentation states why it is one function, at `crates/ferrum-core/src/render/break_axis/mod.rs:1307-1315`: "The one walker both glyph passes share (measure, then move or hide), so a node kind cannot be measured by one rule and rewritten by another. … A measuring pass leaves the node untouched: the only arm that has to *write* (a rect, whose second coordinate is derived) writes only when the closure actually moved something, so measuring cannot drift a rect's width through a `(start + len) - start` round trip." That no-write guarantee is pinned by `measuring_a_glyph_rect_does_not_perturb_its_size` at `crates/ferrum-core/src/render/break_axis/tests.rs:1405-1421`, whose assertion reads "a measuring pass must not write to the node" (line 1420). `#229`(3), the `resolve_break` extraction, was closed with evidence and no code (`42e8c7da`).
- **Three duplicated rules get one owner each, byte-stable by proof (wave 6B, #214, #165(1)(2), #164).** The `compare=` merge that figure functions and visualizers each carried (the dict check, the reserved-`base` refusal and the delegation) lives in one private `ModelSource` helper, and its error texts are unchanged (#214). The `"{name}_{key}"` rule that names a transform's secondary data sources, previously written at three Rust and six Python sites, is one function, `ferrum._core.secondary_key`, called from both languages; the two `_registry.py` column helpers share one validation tail (#165(1)(2)). The 1279-line registry-consumer analyzer inside a test file moves to `tests/_registry_consumer_lint.py` with unit tests of its own and computed counts in place of drifted prose counts (#164). No library output changes.

### Documentation

- `ferrum-spec.md` §3.12 now opens `BreakAxis` with a consolidated current-contract block (parameters, construction/validation vocabulary, render model, per-mark-kind behavior, axis chrome, annotations, facets, same-axis merge, coord refusals, warnings, disclosed residuals). The sixteen dated notes stay below it verbatim as the historical record — several of them retract or supersede each other, so the contract was previously reconstructable only by replaying them in order. The break-axes guide's own Constructor reference caught up too: the `gap` type cell names the temporal and numpy-numeric bounds it accepts, the Validation list covers every construction-time refusal, and the stale "breaks are applied in order, overlaps handled gracefully" sentence is replaced by the union-merge semantics it describes.
- The Gallery page's three classification-diagnostic cards (`docs/site/gallery/img/{roc_chart,pr_chart,calibration_chart}.png`) gained a committed generator, `scripts/generate-gallery-diagnostic-pngs.py`, and were regenerated through it. The 94 gallery images were produced ad hoc in May 2026 with no recorded recipe, so a change to what a diagnostic chart says left them stale with no way to refresh them — which had already bitten once: the calibration card was rendered in May and still showed `Brier = 0.007`, a number produced by the pre-F-L11-04 bin-level path that September replaced with the true per-sample score. The generator's data and split are a **reconstruction** (the originals were not recoverable from the committed PNGs), so the metric values on these three cards moved as well as the duplicate text disappearing: `AUC = 0.974 → 0.885`, `AP = 0.983 → 0.871`, `Brier = 0.007 → 0.136`. Canvas (1160×800), theme (`paper_ink`) and card titles are unchanged.
- The guide's "Legend title" demonstration now shows the feature it demonstrates. `docs/site/guide/marks-encodings.md` has illustrated `fm.Color("species", type="N", title="Species")` since before channel `title=` reached legends, so its rendered image (`img/marks-encodings_02.png`) showed a legend headed `species` — contradicting the paragraph above it. Regenerated under the #90 fix; it now reads `Species`, and the plot rect shifts slightly because the wider title changes the legend's measured width. The page's second `title=` example is prose-only with no rendered image, and the `recipes.md` example uses `fm.Legend(title=...)`, which always worked and whose image is unchanged in this respect. The guide's claim that "the legend title defaults to the field name; override it with the `title=` parameter on the encoding channel" was aspirational when written and is now literally true.
- `ferrum-spec.md` gains dated notes for every wave-4 contract change (#90 and #130 in §3.2, #201-D and the zero-extent-rect rider under `ferrum.Layer`, #193/#194/#200/#201-B under `displot`, #192 under `catplot`, #121 under `pr_chart`), and the two rows that never learned about the rewritten binning parameter now state the accepted vocabulary explicitly: the `mark_histogram(...)` statistical-mark row gains `bins` and `position_by`, and the `stat_bin(...)` row's signature gains `bins=` with its accepted set and its mutual exclusion with `bin_count`/`bin_width` spelled out.
- Corrected two stale claims in the 0.23.0 entry below, both of which were true when written and are superseded by this wave: the x/y-tick-ownership fix's closing sentence, which said closing the label-baseline gap "needs the scale-pixel-range fix tracked as a follow-up, not this one" and called the bottom label's offset "the lone outlier" — the scale-pixel-range fix and the tick-label rigid-translation fix both shipped this wave (#226, above), and no break-axis tick label sits at an offset from its dash different from its siblings' any longer.
- `ferrum-spec.md` gains dated notes for every wave-6A contract change: the break-axis token refusal (§3.12, 2026-09-20), the `cull_threshold`/`cull_labels` split and the independent-x facet fix (§3.13, 2026-09-20), the cleared-paint rule becoming universal and outranking the color channel (§3.2, the color-literal note, 2026-09-21), and the format-predicate unification (§3.7 `Axis`, 2026-09-22).
- `ferrum-spec.md` gains dated notes (2026-09-23) for every wave-6B contract change: `#119` under `ferrum.Layer`, `#123` under the appearance channels and `StrokeDash`, `#128` under §3.10's conditionals and §3.3's `stroke_dash` normalizer, `#149` under `Axis`, `#239` beside the batch-H `Inset` note, and the aggregate vocabulary and `#195` in §3.5. The wave-4 `#193` note's claim that every non-FD `Bin` repr is byte-identical, true when written, carries a correction now that `#195` completes the repr.

## 0.23.0 — 2026-09-11

Three api-contract-audit remediation batches land at once: the figure-helpers batch ([#202](https://github.com/chris-santiago/ferrum/issues/202) — catplot's estimating kinds aggregate for real over seeded-bootstrap CIs, `native_scale=` works both ways, the lmplot/regplot family stops corrupting its own scatter, displot's KDE overlay scales and colors correctly, clustermap dendrograms stop blanking), the diagnostics-correctness batch ([#225](https://github.com/chris-santiago/ferrum/issues/225) — every metric number a diagnostic chart displays now comes from one source of truth: stable-sort AUC/AP, per-sample Brier everywhere, real class names, binary targets outside `{0, 1}` finally computed correctly, string-labeled confusion matrices un-zeroed), and the annotations-interactivity batch ([#246](https://github.com/chris-santiago/ferrum/issues/246) — segment arrows and `annotate_arrow` heads become real, the full `BreakAxis` clipping/placement/suppression contract ships, `Inset` validates and honors `px()`, `Parameter.ref()` and channel `condition=` reach real pipelines under one typed vocabulary, and every composition view learns `to_dict()`/`to_json()`/`pipe()` with lossless figure chrome). Silent parameter drops die loudly throughout; the per-batch sections below carry the full contracts.

Batch F closes eight open api-contract-audit findings (F-L09-01/02/03/04/08/10, F-L10-04/09) plus the F-L10-01 layer-render-robustness rider surfaced along the way. Silent parameter drops in the figure-level helpers die loudly throughout.

### Added

- **Breaking:** `catplot(kind="point"/"bar")` now renders one mean estimate per (category × `hue`) group over a seeded-bootstrap CI whisker, from a single `Summary` transform — previously each drew one mark per raw row (200 overplotted circles for a 200-row frame). `ci=`/`n_boot=` are honored (`ci` a percent in the open interval `(0, 100)`, `n_boot >= 1`), with typed refusals for out-of-range values and for `bool` (`ci=True` used to mean a 1% interval); `seed=` seeds the bootstrap as well as `Jitter`. Both kinds are layered charts now, so `mark=` takes per-layer keys (`"whisker"` plus `"point"`/`"bar"`) instead of a flat mark-kwargs dict. An explicit value-channel `scale=` carrying a `domain` is honored; a domain-less type-only scale (e.g. `{"type": "log"}`) now raises naming the working route instead of silently clipping the CI whiskers. The other six `kind=` values are byte-identical.
- A layered chart's auto-inferred value-axis domain now extends to zero when a bar layer binds that channel (the same zero-anchor a single-chart `mark_bar` already gets), suppressed when the bar binds an explicit span channel (`y2`/`x2`). This is what keeps `catplot(kind="bar")`'s bars on a zero baseline; a per-layer `mark_bar(zero=False)` (or catplot's own `mark={"bar": {"zero": False}}`) opts a layer out, and the opt-out now survives composition into a layer, including `resolve={"y": "independent"}`, `SecondaryY`, and every `.interactive()` `LayerChart`. One phase-10 golden (`class_prediction_error_multiclass.svg`) moved its baseline accordingly.
- `catplot(native_scale=)` is fully implemented both ways. **Breaking:** `native_scale=False` (the default) now forces nominal typing for a numeric category column — previously the category channel was never explicitly typed, so a numeric column (e.g. an integer year) silently inferred a quantitative axis, contradicting the documented default; it now renders as equal-spaced categorical bands, matching a string column and matching seaborn. `native_scale=True` types the channel quantitative and places categories at their true numeric positions, raising a typed error naming the column and dtype for a non-numeric column; the former hard refusal ("the renderer does not support quantitative categorical axes") is deleted — it was factually wrong. An explicit `type_=` on an already-built channel takes precedence over both. `kind="strip"`'s `Jitter` width on a native scale is now data-derived (0.4× the median unique-value spacing) instead of the ordinal band-fraction, and the quantitative domain pads so jitter/dodge offsets never silently drop marks.
- `catplot`'s `hue_order=` is now honored for `kind="box"` and `kind="violin"` — previously silently ignored (byte-identical output regardless of the argument). A bare `Color(sort=...)` on `mark_boxplot`/`mark_violin`/`mark_errorbar`/`mark_errorband` is honored the same way. `kind="boxen"`/`"swarm"` are unaffected (their `hue_order=` already worked through chart-level color).
- Options stated on a channel you build yourself now reach every `catplot` kind. `x=fm.X("cat", sort=[...])` and `hue=fm.Color("g", sort=[...])` were honored by the six per-row kinds but discarded by `kind="point"`/`"bar"`, whose layers rebuilt both channels from bare field names and re-threaded only `type_`, `order=` and the native-scale domain — so the encoding spelling of an order silently did nothing while the `order=`/`hue_order=` kwarg spelling worked. Both spellings now resolve at one site and land in one place, for every option (`sort=`, `title=`, `axis=`, `scale=`, …), on all eight kinds. Where both are given, the kwarg still wins, as it always has. The third door behaves uniformly too: `encode={"x": …}` is a raw override that replaces the builder's normalized category channel — it always did that on the six per-row kinds, and now does it on `kind="point"`/`"bar"` as well instead of being silently inert there. **Breaking:** a per-observation appearance channel on the two estimating kinds now raises instead of drawing a legend for a mapping the marks cannot carry — `catplot(kind="point", size="sz")` drew uniform-radius estimator marks under a six-swatch size legend, and `opacity=` was a byte-identical no-op. `size`, `shape`, `opacity`, `fill_opacity`, `stroke_opacity`, `stroke_width`, `stroke_dash`, `angle`, `color`, `fill` and `stroke` are refused for `kind="point"`/`"bar"` (through both the keyword and the `encode=` dict), naming `hue=` — which joins the `Summary` groupby and therefore does work — and the per-row kinds as the routes. The per-row kinds are unaffected.
- **Fixed:** `lmplot`/`regplot` no longer replace the scatter's observations with the fit line's evaluation grid. With `ci=None` and `show_metrics=False` the fit collapsed to a single line mark bound to a chain-advancing (unnamed) `Smooth`, which then applied to the scatter layer too: every "observation" landed exactly on a fit vertex (measured: 200 of 200, zero spread about the curve) for `lm`, `loess`, `quadratic`, `gam`, `log` and `sqrt`, in both functions — or raised `unknown column` outright when the data's columns were not themselves named `x`/`y`. The fit now binds to a named transform through `data_source=`, the same shape the CI path and the `logistic`/`glm`/`robust` methods already used. On that one combination the fit layer is named `"line"` rather than `"fit"` (matching every other `lmplot` shape), so a `mark={"fit": …}` override for it becomes `mark={"line": …}`. Separately, `x_jitter=` on an integer predictor now renders instead of raising `Jitter: … expected Int64 but found Float64`; that failed on three of the four `ci`/`show_metrics` combinations and was masked on the fourth by the scatter corruption above.
- `displot(kind="kde", cumulative=True)` now produces a real CDF — previously the kwarg was silently dropped for the primary KDE mark and rendered byte-identically to `cumulative=False`. The `kde=True` overlay on `kind="hist"` inherits the same `cumulative` value. That overlay is also count-scaled and per-hue: under `kind="hist", stat="count", multiple != "fill"`, the curve is rescaled to the histogram's own bin width and group size so it's visible against the bars instead of a flat ~2%-height sliver, and it now threads `groupby=hue` with a matching color channel instead of drawing one pooled, uncolored curve over per-hue bars. `stat=` is validated against `{"count", "density"}` for every `kind=`; an unsupported value (e.g. `"probability"`) now raises instead of silently degrading to a count-mode render. `stat="density"` overlays and `kind="ecdf"` are byte-identical. **Fixed:** `rug=True` combined with `hue=` and `multiple="stack"`/`"fill"` no longer crashes (`"Stack: value (y) encoding required"`) — the rug and kde overlay layers now carry an explicit `Identity()` position so neither can inherit a stack/normalize adjustment; rug ticks are never dodged or stacked.
- `clustermap`'s dendrogram panels no longer silently blank at practical ratios — the dendrogram leaves were reserving axis chrome (a title gutter, and on the row side a label band) for axes that `axis(show=False)` never draws. `dendrogram_ratio=0.1` now renders both dendrograms, and the default ratio (`0.2`) no longer blanks the column dendrogram on a short heatmap (e.g. `figsize=(480, 200)`). Sub-floor ratios still raise `PaddingExceedsViewport`. **All clustermap output changes geometry** as a result (dead chrome removed from the dendrogram panels); the `clustermap_basic` and `clustermap_row_col_dendrograms` goldens were regenerated and PNG-inspected. Two adjacent layered-chart wire bugs found alongside it are fixed generally: `title=None` on a layered channel now reaches the wire and suppresses the axis title (a truthiness check was dropping it, same as it already dropped `title=""`); `format=` on a layered channel no longer raises an unknown-field error; a layer's `condition=` now reaches the wire instead of silently vanishing.
- A layered chart with a layer (index ≥ 1) whose positional encoding (`x`/`y`/`x2`/`y2`) names a column missing from that layer's own resolved batch now raises a typed error naming the layer index, channel, and column — previously only layer 0 was validated, and every later layer's missing column silently rendered nothing. An inherited color channel absent from a layer's batch is unaffected (still renders); zero-row layers still skip. The gate surfaced two formerly-invisible bugs, both fixed: the discrimination-threshold desugar no longer draws its optimum-label/threshold-line layers when the f1-gated sentinel columns were never injected, and a secondary-y smooth chart now encodes the transform's real output column.
- `residplot(color=)` works, both documented halves. A value naming a column of `data` groups the residuals (refit per group via the transform's `groupby`, colored by that column, with a `lowess=True` overlay drawing one curve per group) — previously every `color=` value raised `ValueError: unknown column`. A value that is not a column is parsed as a literal color and painted onto the points via mark-level `fill=`, composing with `label=` for a labeled legend swatch in that color; a value that is neither raises, naming both interpretations. `robust=True, label=...` now shows the label (previously a silent no-op). `show_metrics=True`'s corner annotation is suppressed only when `color=` actually groups — a `label=`-only grouping keeps its corner. A user column literally named `_label` plus `label=` now refuses with a typed collision error instead of silently being overwritten.
- `regplot` gains four real named parameters — `logx`, `show_metrics`, `x_bins`, `x_estimator` — with `lmplot`'s exact defaults; previously reachable only through the kwargs bag, invisible to introspection. `regplot(col=...)`/`regplot(row=...)` now raise `TypeError` naming `lmplot` as the faceting route, instead of silently faceting.
- `ferrum.Robust` gains `groupby` (mirroring `Smooth`), enabling per-group Huber-IRLS refits with group-key columns re-emitted at their original dtypes. A `Smooth`/`Robust` `groupby` column that collides with the transform's own output column name now refuses with a typed error naming the real output columns — previously a duplicate-named Arrow field was silently mis-resolved, corrupting output instead of refusing. Separately, a `groupby` list that repeats a column name (`groupby=["g", "g"]`) now refuses across the whole partitioning family — `Bin`, `Kde`, `Kde2D`, `Smooth`, `Robust`, `Outliers`, `TopK`, `Impute`, `RegressionData`, `LoessData` — naming the repeated column. A repeat is a malformed request rather than a property of any one transform, so the refusal moved into the shared partitioner they all call. For those ten, a repeat previously emitted two identically-named output fields, and every downstream reader of "the groupby column" silently resolved to the first of them. (`Aggregate`, `Summary`, `Violin`, `BoxStats`, and `ErrorExtent` already refused the same repeat at construction time and are unchanged.)

### Breaking changes

- `catplot(kind="point"/"bar")` renders aggregated bootstrap estimates by default instead of one mark per raw row (documented behavior — the guide already said this — now actually true); `mark=` on those two kinds takes per-layer keys.
- `catplot(native_scale=False)`, the default, forces nominal typing for numeric category columns (previously silently inferred quantitative).
- All `clustermap` output changes geometry (dead dendrogram chrome removed); `ratio=0.1` and short heatmaps now render their dendrograms.
- New typed refusals of previously-accepted calls: `regplot(col=/row=)` (silently faceted); a layered chart's layer ≥ 1 naming a missing positional column (silently rendered nothing); `catplot(kind="point"/"bar")` with a domain-less type-only value scale (would silently clip CI whiskers); `catplot(kind="point"/"bar")` with a per-observation appearance channel — `size`, `shape`, `opacity`, `fill_opacity`, `stroke_opacity`, `stroke_width`, `stroke_dash`, `angle`, `color`, `fill`, `stroke` (drew a legend over marks that could not carry the mapping, or did nothing at all); `displot(stat=<unsupported>)` (silently degraded to counts); `bool` for `ci=`/`n_boot=`; `Smooth`/`Robust` `groupby` name collisions with an output column, and a repeated `groupby` column name on any partitioning transform (both silently corrupted output); `residplot` with a user column named `_label` plus `label=` (silently overwritten).
- `lmplot`/`regplot` with `ci=None, show_metrics=False` name their fit layer `"line"` instead of `"fit"` (every other shape already did), so a `mark={"fit": …}` override for that one combination becomes `mark={"line": …}`. The rename comes with the fix that stops that combination from overwriting the scatter's observations with the fit grid.
- Layered bar charts' auto-inferred value axis now anchors at zero (one phase-10 golden moved); opt out per layer with `zero=False`.

Batch G closes twelve open api-contract-audit findings in the model-diagnostics surface (F-L11-01/04/05/06/08/09, F-L12-01/02/03/08/09, F-L10-07) plus the `annotate_auc`/`annotate_ap` crash rider surfaced along the way. Every metric number a diagnostic chart displays now comes from one source of truth.

### Added

- `classification_report_chart` gains a `y_true=`/`y_pred=` precomputed path (hard class labels), joining the ten other prediction-evaluation figure functions (`roc_chart`, `pr_chart`, `calibration_chart`, `gain_chart`, `lift_chart`, `discrimination_threshold_chart`, `confusion_matrix_chart`, `class_prediction_error_chart`, `residuals_chart`, `prediction_error_chart`) that already accept it — eleven in total — computed from confusion counts with no sklearn import. The chart now carries a title, `"Classification Report"` (previously untitled), on both paths. The precomputed path never emits a `support` row (a deliberate non-parity with sklearn's own `classification_report`, which does report support counts). An X-less or y-less model-path call now raises a typed `ValueError` naming the diagnostic and the fix instead of leaking a bare sklearn shape error or a foreign `InvalidParameterError`.
- `ModelSource(model, X=None, y=None, ...)` — `X` is now optional (back-compatible). Diagnostics computable from model attributes alone (`pca_variance()` when `explained_variance_ratio_` is present, `intercluster_distance()`) work with no `X`; every other derived-data method raises a typed `ValueError` naming the diagnostic and the fix, replacing a leaked `TypeError` (direct construction) or a leaked `Chart(data=None) requires per-layer data...` error (routed through a figure function's own `X=None` default). A new public, read-only `ModelSource.class_names -> list[str] | None` property is added (`class_names=` → `model.classes_` → `None`; no positional `class_{i}` invention), proxied on `ComparedModelSource`.
- `intercluster_distance_chart(method="tsne")` now renders for every documented `k >= 4` — the perplexity clamp was `min(5, k-1)`, one short of the kNN-size requirement, so `k` in `{4, 5, 6}` raised a typed refusal; it is now `min(5, k-2)`.
- `rank1d_chart(df, y=y, algorithm="covariance")` on a raw DataFrame/2-D array now renders — it previously always routed through a `ModelSource`-shaped path that required `X`, regardless of whether the input was raw tabular data or a fitted estimator.
- `partial_dependence`/`importances(method="permutation")` no longer leak sklearn `UserWarning`s about feature names when the model was fit on a named frame — a pandas DataFrame is rebuilt with the model's own `feature_names_in_` before the sklearn call, with numeric output identical to the numpy path. **Disclosed residual:** a bare-array polars `DataFrame` built by the caller with no `schema=` (auto-generated `column_0, column_1, ...` labels) is treated as a genuinely named frame — polars has no structural marker distinguishing it from a deliberately user-named one — so it now raises sklearn's own feature-name-mismatch error where it previously computed a silently positionally-correct result with a warning. The two most common nameless-matrix shapes, a bare `numpy.ndarray` and `pd.DataFrame(array)`, are unaffected and compute correctly with zero warnings.
- Every `FerrumVisualizer` subclass gains, as explicit keyword-only constructor parameters, every kwarg its underlying chart builder documents — including the override quartet `mark=`/`encode=`/`properties=`/`layers=` on every single visualizer in the family (previously reachable on none of them). `ResidualsVisualizer(panels="auto")` now renders the canonical 4-panel grid, matching `residuals_chart(panels="auto")`, via a shared expansion helper both callers use.

### Fixed

- **Breaking:** every AUC/AP a user sees — the `roc_chart`/`pr_chart` title, the legend text, and the `AUCLabel`/`APLabel` overlay — now reads the same number, sourced from the frame's Rust-kernel-computed `auc`/`ap` column instead of three independent Python re-derivations. The composable `+ AUCLabel()`/`+ APLabel()` path (which has no such column on arbitrary user data) now sorts with a stable sort, since ROC/PR curves routinely repeat one x/recall value across many rows and an unstable sort's within-tie permutation previously changed the integral. PR macro interpolation, gain/lift score ordering, and `ModelSource.silhouette()`'s within-cluster descending sort are all stable too — the last one changes `mark_silhouette`'s rendered bar order for tied silhouette values (user-visible, since `silhouette()` emits `y_position` in row order). AUC/AP values move wherever the old unstable re-derivation was wrong (verified against the kernel column; seven phase-10 goldens regenerated and PNG-inspected); for `per_class=False` averaged titles, the value is now the weighted mean of per-class values (sklearn parity), not a trapezoid over the interpolated averaged curve.
- `mark_roc(annotate_auc=True)`/`mark_pr(annotate_ap=True)` render their metric label instead of raising `layer N: unknown column '_auc_label_x'` (a crash introduced by the batch-F layer-validation gate surfacing a previously-silent no-op).
- **Breaking:** the `BrierLabel` overlay on `calibration_chart` now shows the exact same per-sample Brier score as the title, invariant to `n_bins=`/`strategy=` — it previously integrated the chart's own *binned* reliability-diagram data (not the Brier score at all; observed swinging 0.078 → 0.028 on `strategy=` alone while the true value held at 0.096). The compared-path (`compare=`) overlay now shows each model's own true per-sample Brier instead of one bin-level number shared by every model (supersedes the prior spec's bin-level sanction for the compared path — see the dated `ferrum-spec.md` note). The precomputed path (`calibration_chart(y_true=, y_pred=)`) now carries the Brier in its title too, where it previously stayed blank.
- **Breaking:** `gain_chart`/`lift_chart` show the model's real class names for every class, not a synthetic `"Class N"` rename for the first two — binary charts now label classes `"0"`/`"1"` (the stringified real value) instead of `"Class 0"`/`"Class 1"`, and a 3rd-and-later class (previously unlabeled relative to its siblings) now renders with its own real name too. String-named classes were already rendered as-is and are unaffected. The synthetic baseline row still renames to `"Baseline"`.
- Unknown constructor kwargs on any `FerrumVisualizer` subclass now raise `TypeError` naming the kwarg — the base class's silent `**_extra` swallow is removed.
- `ROCVisualizer`/`PRVisualizer`/`DiscriminationThresholdVisualizer`'s derived metrics (`auc_mean`, `ap_mean`, `best_threshold`/`best_f1`, `__repr__`) under `compare=` now always describe the base model, consistent with `.score()` — previously they could report a value pooled across (or drawn from) a compared rival model while `.score()` reported the base model's own number.
- **Breaking:** a binary target's *labels* no longer change its metrics. Every binary classification diagnostic now binarizes `y` against the positive class before the Rust kernels see it — the kernels count positives by summing `y_true`, so they need a 0/1 indicator, and the binary arms of `roc_curve`/`pr_curve` plus all of `calibration_curve` used to hand them the raw target. Only `y ∈ {0, 1}` was ever correct. `y ∈ {-1, 1}` (the SVM convention) rendered `"ROC Curve — AUC -1.747"` with a `tpr` axis reaching -4.375 against a true AUC of 0.9994; `y ∈ {1, 2}` rendered `AUC 0.302` against 0.9989 and a Brier score of 1.035 (a Brier score is bounded in `[0, 1]`); string class labels raised `could not convert string to float` outright, from `roc_chart`, `pr_chart`, `calibration_chart` and the precomputed `y_true=`/`y_pred=` path alike. All of these now produce exactly the numbers the `{0, 1}` labelling produces for the same probabilities. `{0, 1}` targets are byte-identical (no golden moved). The precomputed path additionally stops comparing `y_true` against the string `"1"`: its two class values are read from `y_true` in `y_true`'s own dtype, and a degenerate single-class `y_true` now labels its curve with the class actually present rather than a fabricated `"1"`.
- `calibration_chart(...) + fm.BrierLabel()` — the example in `BrierLabel`'s own docstring — now renders the true per-sample Brier score, agreeing with the title, on both `strategy="uniform"` and `strategy="quantile"`. It previously rendered the *bin-level* integral (0.030 uniform / 0.009 quantile against a true 0.028 on a reference fixture, a 3.3× swing from a binning kwarg the Brier score is invariant to) **and overwrote** the correct label `annotate_brier=True` had already placed, so following the docstring produced a chart strictly less correct than the default. `ModelSource.calibration_curve()` gains a broadcast `brier` column for this (schema change, additive), computed where the per-sample predictions still exist; title, figure overlay and composable label now all read that one column. Composing `+ AUCLabel()`/`+ APLabel()` onto a `roc_chart`/`pr_chart` reads the frame's `auc`/`ap` column by the same rule — those values already agreed, so nothing rendered moves.
- **Breaking:** a metric column the kernel computed as NaN now displays as `nan` instead of silently falling back to integrating the curve. "The frame carries no such metric" and "this metric is undefined for this curve" are different facts; collapsing them turned the one honest signal a degenerate single-class curve emits into a plausible-looking fabricated number. Frames that carry no metric column at all (arbitrary user data under `+ AUCLabel()`) still integrate, unchanged.
- `CalibrationVisualizer`'s `calibration_error` is now per-model when several models are overlaid — one `calibration_error[<name>]` entry each, where a single pooled scalar over every model's rows previously described no model in particular (while an inline comment claimed per-model values). Single-model visualizers are unchanged.
- `_parallel_coords_chart_from_dataframe`'s dead `alpha=0.3` default is now `0.5`, matching both of its callers and both public surfaces; the visualizer↔chart parity harness now diffs **defaults** as well as parameter names, with the same rationale-carrying, staleness-checked skip-map the name diff uses (two documented entries: `ResidualsVisualizer`/`CooksDistanceVisualizer` `panels=`).
- **Fixed (found by the close's own design review):** a binary classifier that exposes no `classes_` — any hand-rolled estimator, any wrapper that forwards `predict_proba` without re-exporting sklearn's attributes — produced collapsed metrics on every binary diagnostic. The positive class was resolved by parsing the display label back out of the `proba_<label>` column name, but those names come from `class_names`, which is either a user-supplied alias or a synthetic `class_{i}`; neither is a value `y` contains, so the (now mandatory) binarization zeroed the indicator. Measured against a true AUC of 0.9417: `roc_chart` titled `AUC nan`, `pr_curve()["ap"]` was `0.0`, `calibration_curve()["fraction_positive"]` was flat at `0.0`, and the Brier read `0.380` — `mean(p²)`, in range and plausible and wrong. The positive class is now read from `y`'s own distinct values when the model offers no `classes_`, the same rule the precomputed path already used; models that *do* expose `classes_` (including with a `class_names=` display alias) are unaffected.
- **Breaking:** a binary diagnostic whose target matches *no* row of the positive class now raises a typed `ValueError` naming both the class value searched for and the values `y` actually holds, instead of drawing a curve against an entirely-negative phantom target. An all-null target refuses the same way (nulls compare unequal to everything). This covers `roc_curve`, `pr_curve`, `calibration_curve` and `discrimination_threshold`; multiclass one-vs-rest is deliberately unaffected, since "no row is class k" is a legitimate answer there and `lift_curve` documents skipping zero-base-rate classes. An empty target is not an error, and an all-*positive* target still reports an honest NaN from the kernels. The refusal judges the request, not an internal slice of it: `discrimination_threshold(cv=...)` validates the positive class once against the whole target and then sweeps folds from that validated indicator, so a held-out fold containing no positives still contributes its honest all-zero sweep (unchanged numbers) instead of aborting the call.
- **Fixed (found by the close's own dtype matrix):** `confusion_matrix()` — and therefore `confusion_matrix_chart` and `ConfusionMatrixVisualizer` — rendered an **all-zero matrix** whenever `y` was `Utf8`, `Categorical`, or `Enum` while the model's `classes_` was numeric (the ordinary shape for a string-labelled target fed to any sklearn classifier: `predict` returns `classes_`'s dtype, not `y`'s). The encode values were correctly coerced into `y`'s domain, but the *predictions* were left in `classes_`'s, so every `(actual, predicted)` pair encoded to the kernel's out-of-vocabulary code and was dropped. Predictions are now translated through the same positional `classes_ -> y`-domain correspondence the encoding uses. Numeric `y` is byte-identical (the translation is the identity there).
- Documentation corrections: the visualizer-family module docstring no longer advertises a `.predict` / `get_params` / `set_params` surface and `GridSearchCV` composability that does not exist (the real surface is `fit` / `score` / `has_score` / `show`, and without `get_params` `sklearn.base.clone` cannot work); `ModelSource`'s constructor docstring no longer promises a `class_names` fallback to "the unique values of `y`", which the property has never done and documents at length why.

### Breaking changes

- AUC/AP values in titles/legends/overlays change wherever ties made the old unstable sort wrong; averaged-curve titles change number semantics to the weighted mean of per-class values.
- Compared-path `BrierLabel`s switch bin-level → per-sample; precomputed calibration gains a Brier in the title.
- Binary targets outside `{0, 1}` — `{-1, 1}`, `{1, 2}`, string class labels — now produce correct ROC/PR/calibration metrics where they previously produced fabricated ones or raised; `{0, 1}` output is byte-identical.
- `chart + fm.BrierLabel()` shows the per-sample Brier instead of the bin-level integral, and no longer replaces a correct existing label; `ModelSource.calibration_curve()` gains a `brier` column.
- A NaN metric column renders as `nan` instead of falling back to an integrated (fabricated) value.
- `confusion_matrix()` on a string/categorical `y` returns real counts instead of an all-zero matrix.
- Binary diagnostics on a model without `classes_` return correct metrics instead of collapsed ones (`nan` AUC, `0.0` AP, a `mean(p²)` Brier).
- A binary target matching no positive row, or an all-null target, now raises a typed `ValueError` naming the class value and y's values instead of silently producing a plausible number.
- `CalibrationVisualizer._metrics`/`__repr__` key multi-model calibration error per model (`calibration_error[<name>]`) instead of reporting one pooled `calibration_error`.
- Gain/lift binary labels: `"Class 0"`/`"Class 1"` → the model's real class names (`"0"`/`"1"` for integer classes).
- Unknown `FerrumVisualizer` constructor kwargs go from silently swallowed to `TypeError`.
- `classification_report_chart` gains a title and a new precomputed path; `ModelSource`'s `X` becomes optional with typed refusals replacing leaked internal errors.
- A bare-array polars `DataFrame` (`pl.DataFrame(array)`, no `schema=`) fed to a `ModelSource` whose model was fit under different real feature names now raises sklearn's feature-name-mismatch error instead of silently computing a positionally-correct result with a warning.

Batch H closes seventeen open api-contract-audit findings across annotations, structural charts, the reactive-parameter surface, and the composite/output API (F-L08-01/02/03/06/07/08, F-L13-01/02/03, F-L14-01/02/03/04, F-L15-01, F-L06-01/02/04). Dormant kwargs and dead spec-fiction surfaces either become real or get their docs corrected to match reality — nothing stays silently half-true.

### Added

- `mark_segment(arrow=True, arrow_size=8.0)` now draws a filled triangle arrowhead at `(x2, y2)` — a documented kwarg pair that previously raised `TypeError`. `mark_segment()` without `arrow=` is byte-identical.
- `Inset(bounds=...)` accepts `fm.px(...)` entries without raising — pixel-tagged bounds resolve against the parent plot rect at layout time, per the original design `px()` had drifted away from. Mixed `px()`/`norm()`/bare-float entries in one bounds tuple resolve independently.
- `InteractiveChart.to_html()` returns the byte-identical string twin of `.interactive().save("x.html")`, matching the guide's claim — the method didn't exist before.
- A channel's own `condition=<ConditionalSpec>` kwarg (`Color(field=..., condition=selection.when(...).otherwise(...))`) now reaches the real conditional pipeline instead of serializing into an inert per-channel wire blob no renderer consumed. One vocabulary governs both spellings of a conditional (`encode(<channel>=cond)` and `<Channel>(field, condition=cond)`): it may target `color`, `size`, `opacity`, `stroke_width`, `stroke_opacity`, `stroke_dash`, `fill_opacity` or `angle`, and `fill` carries it onto `color` — the same target it aliases to for its base encoding, so both spellings produce the identical wire conditional. `stroke` and `shape` are **not** conditional targets, each for a measured renderer reason: the only conditional colour value repaints a mark's fill, so a stroke conditional would have recoloured the interior instead of the outline; and there is no shape value at all, so a shape conditional serialized cleanly and then did nothing. Both raise the same typed error as any other non-conditional channel until `EncodingValue::StrokeColor` / `EncodingValue::Shape` exist (filed follow-ups). `StrokeWidth`, `StrokeOpacity`, `FillOpacity` and `Angle` now declare `condition` honored, so their working `condition=` no longer draws an "accepted but not yet honored" warning. A wire channel carries at most one conditional: two declarations that resolve to the same channel (e.g. `color=` and `fill=`) now raise, naming both spellings, instead of emitting two conditionals with no defined winner.
- `sel.when(...)`/`.otherwise(...)` branches accept a bare string as field shorthand (parsed the same grammar `encode()` uses) — a bare string means a field everywhere else in ferrum, and this closes the one place it didn't; a literal still requires `fm.value(...)`.
- Every composition view (`HConcatChart`, `VConcatChart`, `ConcatChart`, `LayerChart`, `JointChart`, `RepeatChart`, `ClusterMapChart`) gains `to_dict()`, `to_json(*, indent=None)`, and `pipe(fn, *args, **kwargs)`; `Chart` gains `pipe()`. `to_dict()` is a recursive declaration dict — a nested composite child now embeds recursively instead of `None`. The six `_CompositeBase` views carry the figure-level `title`/`subtitle`/`caption` set via `.properties(...)` under those key names, omitted when unset (so a chrome-free composition's dict is unchanged, and `JointChart`/`RepeatChart`/`ClusterMapChart`'s `to_dict()` still equals `.spec` when untitled); `LayerChart` carries `title` only, since a single-plot overlay has one title slot and fans `subtitle`/`caption` to its layers. A `title` is serialized through the same helper a leaf chart's title uses, so it carries the same `{"text": ...}` shape at every level of the dict and `fm.Title(...)` no longer produces a dict `to_json()` cannot serialize.
- `Facet(field, columns=N)` (the `facet=` wrap channel) is honored — previously a warn-and-noop. It writes the same `ncols` sink `Chart.facet(ncols=N)` already writes, with last-writer-wins precedence between the two spellings. `FacetRow`/`FacetCol`'s `columns=` is unaffected (still warns and drops — a wrap count has no meaning in grid mode).
- **Breaking (close cycle 3, H3-1):** `Chart.layer(Layer(encoding={...}))`'s channel-carried `condition=` now reaches the real conditional pipeline too — a fourth entry point alongside `encode()`'s two spellings and `Chart.conditional()`, gated by the same vocabulary (`stroke`/`shape`/`x`/`y` raise the same typed error). Previously it silently serialized into the dead per-layer `EncodingSpec.condition` blob and did nothing.

### Fixed

- **Breaking:** `annotate_arrow(...)` now renders a filled arrowhead by default on all three of its render paths — standalone `.to_svg()`, unlabelled `+` overlay, and labelled overlay — where it previously rendered headless everywhere; `ann.arrow()` (which already drew a head) is unchanged. Both `mark_segment(arrow=True)` and `annotate_arrow` share one triangle-geometry helper, so the two are byte-consistent for the same endpoints and size.
- **Breaking:** `annotate_vline(..., label=...)`'s label now renders near the top of the line, matching its docstring — it previously anchored to the plot's bottom edge under `NormCoord`'s real y convention.
- `NormCoord`'s docstring corrected: `0.0` is the left/top edge and `1.0` is the right/bottom edge (SVG's downward y), not left/bottom → right/top as it previously (and always incorrectly) claimed. No render-side behavior changed — only the docs were wrong.
- **Breaking:** `BreakAxis` marks, glyphs, and ticks now follow the guide's documented clipping/placement contract instead of silently distorting under a gap. Extent marks (bar/area/rect/ribbon, plus filled shapes like violin) clip per visible segment instead of stretching across the gap or vanishing; glyph marks (point/tick/text/label/image) translate whole to their compressed position and hide whole when their anchor falls in a gap, never split or resized; vertex-placed marks (line/rule/segment/polygon-outline/arc) place each vertex independently. In-gap ticks and gridlines hide correctly, the axis title no longer teleports off-canvas when a gap straddles the axis midpoint (three existing break goldens re-blessed and PNG-inspected — one element each, the returned title), a `SecondaryY` layer is exempt from a break declared on the primary axis, and gap normalization fixes an indicator-pixel accounting bug at gaps touching a domain edge.
- **Breaking:** a `BreakAxis` annotation whose data-space coordinate falls inside a gap is now suppressed with a named warning instead of silently rendering at the unbroken (wrong) position; an extent-shaped annotation (rect, span, line, arrow, bracket) is suppressed when its interval *crosses* a gap too, not only when an endpoint lands in one — previously a rect large enough to contain the gap came back and painted straight across the break while every smaller, overlapping rect was dropped. A surviving data-space annotation is remapped through every same-axis break the same way a mark would be. Separately, and on **every** chart rather than only broken ones: an out-of-domain annotation value now clamps to the correct plot edge (a prior sign error put it on the mirrored side, e.g. `annotate_hline(y=-50)` on a `[0, 100]` axis rendered above the plot; the break's fold only made the long-standing error conspicuous). `Inset(connect_to=...)` resolves its out-of-domain anchor through that same corrected rule.
- `BreakAxis` no longer disappears without a trace. A break on a reversed scale (`LinearScale(reverse=True)`, whose domain is descending) now actually breaks the axis instead of rendering an unbroken chart; a break that resolves to zero effective gaps (every gap outside the domain, or an ordinal axis, which has no numeric domain for a gap to clip against) warns instead of silently doing nothing, and draws no break indicator over a scale it never moved; and a break on a non-linear scale (`log`, `pow`/`sqrt`, `symlog`) warns that its gap placement is computed with a linear pixel-to-data map and so removes a different data range than requested (unfixed, and previously silent).
- `mark_segment(arrow=True)` under a `BreakAxis` keeps its arrowhead's size and its alignment with the shaft: the head is now rebuilt from the shaft's placed endpoints — the same thing `annotate_arrow` has always done on the annotation path — instead of having its three vertices placed independently, which sheared a 20px head into a ~90px one across a gap. The shaft still places per vertex.
- An x-axis's tick marks survive a y-axis `BreakAxis` (and vice versa). A break decided which chrome was "its own" from a pixel coordinate, and a downward x tick starts ON the plot floor — inside the y axis's own pixel span — so on a chart with no scale padding all of its x ticks were reverse-mapped and clamped to a single pixel, i.e. rendered as zero-length lines. Ownership now comes from the axis's own dimension. Affected goldens (three break-indicator charts and the clipped-bar chart) were regenerated: one element each, the bottom y tick label's baseline, which moved 4.6px closer to its siblings' convention — every other y label on a broken axis sits +4.63px below its own dash, and the bottom label now sits at +0.00px rather than its prior −4.33px offset. It remains the lone outlier; the break's segments are laid over the plot rect while the label's baseline offset reverse-maps as if it were a data position, and closing that gap needs the scale-pixel-range fix tracked as a follow-up, not this one.
- **Breaking:** a `BreakAxis` whose gaps all miss the (padded) domain — the "zero effective gaps" case just above — now renders exactly the unbroken chart, not merely a stable approximation of one. The zero-effective-gaps warning was accurate about the *diagnosis* from the moment it shipped, but the render still went through a single-segment "identity" map laid over the plot rect while the real remap reverse-maps from the scale's own PADDED pixel range — so it silently collapsed the scale's padding, moving every mark, tick, and gridline by up to the padding width (as much as 8px on a typical chart) on a break the warning itself says did nothing. Dropping that stale map closes the gap between the diagnosis and the render; no goldens move (none of the shipped break goldens have out-of-domain gaps on a continuous axis).
- `Inset(bounds=...)` now refuses at construction time for out-of-`[0, 1]` normalized entries, negative pixel entries, and an empty box (compared only within like kinds) — previously these constructed silently and rendered off-canvas or degenerate. The docstring and guide drop the incorrect "bare float = data-space" claim; bare floats have always meant normalized `[0, 1]` fractions.
- `fm.param(...).ref()` is now consumed at all three documented sites — scale `domain=`, `transform_filter(...)`, and a conditional's `when()`/`otherwise()` branch — instead of silently going nowhere, or, at `transform_filter`, silently mangling into a garbage predicate. A conditional ref resolves to the named parameter's registered static value on a real chart, or raises `ValueError` naming the parameter when it isn't a registered value-bearing `fm.param()`.
- Auto-raster's color exclusion now splits by type, and the type is inferred from the column dtype when the channel carries no annotation — `color="z"` on a numeric column substitutes exactly like `color="z:Q"`, `color="c"` on a string column stays excluded, and an explicit annotation always wins (`color="z:N"` on a numeric column keeps the exclusion). The exclusion warning describes only the type the gate actually determined, so it never calls a numeric column categorical. A **quantitative** color encoding no longer blocks auto-raster — it substitutes `mark_raster(aggregate="mean", field=<color field>, cmap=<the channel's own scheme, or raster_scheme>)` instead of silently staying vector past `raster_threshold`. A **categorical/ordinal/temporal** color encoding keeps the exclusion (permanently — no pixel aggregate preserves a category's identity), now with a specific warning naming the field and the manual `mark_raster(...)` workaround. An explicit `RenderConfig(raster_aggregate=..., raster_field=...)` still wins wholesale over the color-driven default.
- **Breaking:** every composite constructor (`HConcatChart`, `VConcatChart`, `ConcatChart`, `LayerChart`, `JointChart`, `RepeatChart`, `ClusterMapChart`) now raises `TypeError` naming the offending type for a child that isn't a `Chart` or composition object, instead of constructing silently and failing much later at render time with a generic node-kind error. `chart | non_chart` / `chart & non_chart` now raise Python's own operator `TypeError` instead of building a broken composite around the invalid operand.
- **Fixed (close cycle 3/4, H3-4):** a composition's `.properties(title=..., subtitle=..., caption=...)` now actually renders for every one of the three chrome keys, on all seven wrappers — `to_svg()`/`.interactive()` previously raised an unmapped `TypeError: 'Title' object is not an instance of 'str'` on `subtitle=`/`caption=fm.Title(...)`, a spelling `to_dict()`/`to_json()` silently accepted. `title=fm.Title(text=..., subtitle=...)` now routes the `Title`'s own `subtitle` into the composite's separate (already-working) subtitle slot instead of silently dropping it, unless an explicit `subtitle=` is *also* given in the same call, which raises `ValueError` naming both spellings. A `Title` field with genuinely nowhere to go on a composite (`anchor`, `offset`, `font_size`, `font_weight`, `color`, `subtitle_font_size`, `subtitle_color`; `subtitle` itself on `subtitle=`/`caption=`, or on `LayerChart`'s `title=`, which has no separate subtitle slot) raises `ValueError` at `.properties()` time rather than silently vanishing. A leaf `Chart`'s own `title=` is unaffected and keeps full `Title` richness.
- **Fixed (close cycle 4):** `encode(color=fm.Color("g"), fill=fm.Fill(condition=cond))` — a fieldless `fill` conditioning `color` alongside a base `color` encoding — builds again. Cycle 3's alias-collision guard raised on this call unconditionally; a fieldless alias source has no field for the alias to drop, so there was nothing to collide with. The equivalent bare-`ConditionalSpec` spelling (`encode(color=..., fill=cond)`) was never affected.
- **Fixed (close cycle 4):** `Chart.layer(Layer(encoding={"color": cond}))` — the bare `ConditionalSpec` spelling inside a layer's encoding, matching `encode(color=cond)` — now reaches the chart's real conditional pipeline instead of silently vanishing (no warning, no conditional, the key simply absent from the serialized layer). Cycle 3 only routed the channel-carried spelling (`Layer(encoding={"color": fm.Color(f, condition=cond)})`).

### Breaking changes

- `annotate_arrow(...)` renders a filled arrowhead by default on every render path; `annotate_vline(..., label=...)`'s label moves from the bottom to the top of the line.
- `BreakAxis` extent/glyph/tick placement changes to match the documented clipping model (three existing break goldens moved by one element — the restored axis title); in-gap annotations now suppress with a warning instead of rendering at the wrong position, and surviving annotations remap through the break.
- A `BreakAxis` whose gaps all miss the (padded) axis domain now renders exactly the unbroken chart instead of an up-to-8px-shifted approximation of one — no goldens move (no shipped break golden has out-of-domain gaps on a continuous axis).
- `Inset(bounds=...)` raises `ValueError` at construction for out-of-range, negative-pixel, or empty-box entries that previously constructed silently.
- A channel's `condition=` kwarg that isn't a `ConditionalSpec` now raises `TypeError` instead of being silently accepted and serialized into a dead wire blob. A conditional declared on a channel that cannot carry one (`text`, `tooltip`, `detail`, `href`, `key`, `url`, `description`, the facet channels, and the positional channels) raises `ValueError` at `encode()` time naming the supported set — before this batch such a call was silently inert.
- Charts past `raster_threshold` whose `color` channel names a numeric column with no explicit type now substitute `mark_raster` (previously they stayed vector and warned, on the mistaken grounds that an unannotated column is categorical).
- `LayerChart.to_dict()` omits `title` when no title is set, instead of emitting `"title": None` — the same omit-when-unset rule the other six composition views now follow. A composition's `to_dict()` `title` is a `{"text": ...}` dict (the leaf chart's own title shape), not a bare string.
- `stroke` and `shape` are not conditional targets: `encode(stroke=<ConditionalSpec>)`, `encode(shape=<ConditionalSpec>)`, and the `Stroke(field, condition=...)` / `Shape(field, condition=...)` spellings all raise `ValueError`. `fill` and `stroke_dash` are unaffected. A `shape` conditional previously built and rendered — it just had no visual effect.
- Two conditionals resolving to the same wire channel (e.g. `encode(color=condA, fill=condB)`, or a second `Chart.conditional()` on an already-conditioned channel) raise `ValueError` instead of both reaching the wire.
- Composite constructors and `|`/`&` operators raise `TypeError` for a non-`Chart`, non-composition child/operand instead of constructing silently.
- Quantitative-color charts past `raster_threshold` now substitute `mark_raster` instead of silently staying vector.
- **(close cycle 3/4)** `Chart.layer(Layer(encoding={"color": cond}))` (both the bare `ConditionalSpec` and channel-carried spellings) now lands the conditional in the chart's real `conditionals` pipeline instead of the dead per-layer wire blob; a `stroke`/`shape`/`x`/`y` conditional inside a layer's encoding now raises the same typed `ValueError` `encode()` raises.
- **(close cycle 3/4)** `encode(color=fm.Color("d"), fill=fm.Fill("c", condition=cond))` (and the layer-encoding equivalent) now raises `ValueError` when `fill` carries a field — previously it silently dropped `fill`'s field while keeping its conditional, which then overrode `color` at selection time. A fieldless `fill=fm.Fill(condition=cond)` is unaffected and still builds.
- **(close cycle 3/4)** A composition's `.properties(title=..., subtitle=..., caption=...)` now renders for all three chrome keys on all seven wrappers; a `Title`'s own `subtitle` routes into the composite's subtitle slot (or is refused, typed, if it collides with an explicit `subtitle=`); a `Title` field with no composite-chrome slot (`anchor`/`offset`/`font_size`/`font_weight`/`color`/`subtitle_font_size`/`subtitle_color`, or `subtitle` on `subtitle=`/`caption=`/`LayerChart`'s `title=`) raises `ValueError` instead of silently vanishing. A leaf `Chart`'s own `title=` is unaffected and keeps full `Title` richness.
- **(close cycle 4)** `Chart + Chart` now raises `ValueError` when both sides carry a conditional on the same wire channel (e.g. both condition `color`) — previously it built with two conditionals stamped the same channel and no defined winner (the WASM runtime applies every conditional to every mark batch in every panel, with no per-layer scope). Two layers conditioning *different* channels still compose freely.

## 0.22.0 — 2026-09-07

Two correctness campaigns land at once: the transform-engine batch ([#168](https://github.com/chris-santiago/ferrum/issues/168) — one dtype policy across ~20 transforms, live `groupby=` for regression/loess/impute/top_k, tie-aware ranks and peer-aware window frames, an expression grammar that parses its own documented forms, real `glm`/`gam` smoothers, and a Rust-owned `transform_registry` that Python derives from instead of mirroring) and the mark-geometry batch ([#177](https://github.com/chris-santiago/ferrum/issues/177) — real `monotone`/`basis`/`cardinal` curves on line/area/ribbon, arc corner fillets, `mark_function(clip=False)`, standalone `bin=True` with per-bin aggregation, facet strip titles, coord guards, and a boxen/violin/boxplot/contour truth pack). Silent parameter drops die loudly throughout.

### Added

- **Breaking** (close-review hardening): `mark_smooth(method="gam")` gains `k=` (basis dimension, default 10, clamped to `[4, distinct x count]` — the knob for under-smoothing on high-frequency data; refuses `k < 4` and `k=` on non-gam methods). Explicit `bandwidth=` on a non-loess `mark_smooth` method now raises typed instead of being silently ignored (matching `degree=`'s policy). `ferrum._core.transform_registry(kind)` gains a fourth key, `"secondary_outputs"` — the named secondary batches (qq reference line, letter-value bands/outliers, linkage dendrogram) are now declared schema, not unchecked literals; the name-prefix rule is uniform across all three, which changes one runtime key: a `ferrum.QQ(name=...)` spec's reference-line batch is now published as `"{name}_qq_line"` instead of the bare `"qq_line"` (unnamed specs are unchanged). Two formerly-silent aggregate-name fallbacks in `pivot` and `top_k` (an unknown aggregate quietly summing) now raise typed errors.

- **Breaking:** `rank` and `dense_rank` written without a `field` are now tie-aware (behavior change). A window op written the way the guide writes it, `{"op": "rank", "as": "rank"}`, used to fall back to `row_number`, so tied sort values drew distinct, order-dependent ranks. Ranks now come from the `sort` columns: rows equal on every sort column share a rank, competition-style for `rank` (1, 1, 3) and dense-style for `dense_rank` (1, 1, 2), matching SQL and Vega. An explicit `field=` keeps its field-keyed behavior unchanged, and a spec with no `sort=` still ranks by row order.

- **Breaking:** Rolling window frames are peer-aware by default (behavior change). `transform_window(..., frame=...)` now treats rows tied on the `sort` key as a unit: the frame is anchored on the unit's own row-offset bounds and then *both* ends of that range grow outward to whole peer groups, so tied rows always share one frame and therefore one aggregate value and a frame boundary never splits a peer group. Vega's `ignorePeers: false` default also expands both ends outward via `Window.js` `adjustRange` (`bisect.left`/`bisect.right`), but diverges in its anchor: Vega anchors each row's frame on the current row (`i0 = max(0, i - preceding)`, `i1 = min(n, i + following + 1)`), whereas ferrum anchors on the peer run's bounds. This guarantees ferrum's tied rows always share one frame and one value; Vega's need not. Example: on a sort key `t=[1,1,2,3,3]` with values `x=[10,20,30,40,50]` and `frame=(-1,0)`, ferrum outputs `[30,30,60,120,120]` while Vega outputs `[30,30,60,120,90]` — only the last row differs. Pass the new `transform_window(..., ignore_peers=True)` to restore the previous row-offset frames. Only rolling aggregates with an explicit `frame` over a sort key that actually has ties are affected; all other window output is byte-identical.

- The expression grammar (`transform_filter`/`transform_calculate`) gains array literals (`[e1, e2, …]`), function-call syntax, and one builtin, `indexof(array, value)` — the documented membership form `indexof([1, 2, 3], datum.id) >= 0` now actually parses; it previously raised on the `,` inside the array. `transform_filter`'s dict-list shorthand (`{"field": [...]}`) compiles to exactly this form. **Fixed:** a dict/scalar filter value that was a Python `bool` or `None` previously leaked Python's capitalized `repr()` (`True`, `None`) into the generated predicate, which the grammar cannot parse (`unexpected identifier: 'True'`) — `fm.transform_filter({"active": True})` now works. All three dict-filter branches also normalize numpy/pandas scalars (`np.int64`, `np.float64`, `np.bool_`) like their Python-typed equivalents and refuse non-finite floats and unsupported types by name.

- `ferrum._core.transform_registry(kind: str) -> dict` — a new Rust-owned registry giving the method vocabulary, output schema, and accepted parameters for any transform kind (`ValueError` naming the known kinds otherwise). Several previously hand-maintained Python mirrors (`mark_smooth`'s method validation, KDE kernel and QQ distribution vocabularies, output-column names used by figure/desugar consumers) now derive from it instead of risking drift from the Rust implementation.

- `transform_top_k(groupby=...)` — top-k per category: the aggregate (`op` over `field`) is computed per groupby key, the keys are ranked, and every row belonging to a top-`n` key survives. Without `groupby=` the behavior is byte-identical to before (the field's own distinct values double as the group key — a documented historical quirk, not the per-category use case). A row whose groupby key is null belongs to no group and is dropped from grouped output; a bare-string `groupby` raises (iterating a string yields characters, not column names).

- **Breaking:** `lmplot`/`regplot` now draw real fit lines and CI bands for `method="logistic"`, `"glm"`, and `"robust"` — previously those branches referenced a nonexistent `fitted` column and raised (or rendered no fit line with `scatter=True`). `lmplot`'s `method=` vocabulary widened from 5 to 10 names: the smooth-family set (`lm`, `loess`, `quadratic`, `cubic`, `log`, `sqrt`, `gam`) now derives live from the transform registry, with `glm` still mapped to the dedicated GLM transform (its own `family=`/`link=` surface). For `logistic`/`glm`/`robust` with any group-color encoding (`hue=`, or any channel the encoding alias table resolves to color — `color=`/`fill=`/`stroke=`, as keywords or via `encode=`), the fit is pooled across groups (those transforms have no per-group fitting yet) and is drawn in a neutral theme stroke rather than a group color. Four formerly-silent parameter drops now act: `order=`'s default changed `1` → `None` ("the method's own default degree") and any explicit value — including `order=1` — forwards into every smooth-routed method's degree-acceptance matrix (`quadratic`/`cubic` accept their own degree and raise on conflicts; `log`/`sqrt`/`gam` raise since they have no degree concept; `loess` takes it as the local-fit degree, so `order=1` now gives the linear local fit it asks for; negative values raise typed), `truncate=` forwards to `robust` and raises typed for `logistic`/`glm` (which have no extent concept), `show_metrics=` works for every smooth-routed method plus `robust` instead of only `lm`, and `x_bins=`/`x_estimator=` now bin/estimate for every smooth-routed method instead of only `lm` — a chart passing them with e.g. `method="loess"` renders differently (previously they were silently dropped); for `logistic`/`glm`/`robust` (whose dedicated transforms take no binning parameters) an explicit `x_bins=`/`x_estimator=` now raises typed like `truncate=` does, instead of being silently ignored.

- KDE kernels `"exponential"` (`exp(−|u|)/2`) and `"linear"` (triangular, `1−|u|`) join the one shared kernel dispatch, so `mark_density(kernel=...)`'s documented list now computes end to end; `mark_qq(distribution="lognormal")` produces real lognormal theoretical quantiles (it was documented but refused by a stale validation tuple); and `transform_density` gains `kernel=` (default `"gaussian"`, sharing the same dispatch — its gaussian output moves on the order of 1e-15 relative from a summation-order change, invisible at SVG precision).

- **Breaking:** `mark_smooth(method="glm")` is newly accepted and fits a real GLM (IRLS via the shared `glm` core, Gaussian/Identity default), and `mark_smooth(method="gam")` is newly accepted and fits a real generalized additive model — a penalized cubic B-spline (P-spline) with a second-order difference penalty and a smoothing parameter chosen by generalized cross-validation, with the spline's Bayesian posterior confidence band. Both strings previously refused as unknown methods, so no existing chart changes. `gam` bends to follow the data the way `loess` does but is fully deterministic: it uses no randomness at all, so `seed=` has no effect on its band. It takes no `degree=` (the spline degree is fixed at 3; smoothness comes from the penalty), and it holds its boundary value outside the observed x range rather than extrapolating a cubic. `method="lm"` with `degree>=2` now routes to genuine polynomial regression. Formerly-silent paths now raise: `degree=` passed to `log`/`sqrt` (previously ignored; `glm` refuses it too), a `degree=` that conflicts with `quadratic`/`cubic`'s own degree (previously ignored — the error names `method="lm", degree=d` as the escape hatch; a redundant matching degree is still accepted), and a whole-frame polynomial `degree` at or above the usable point count (previously rendered an empty plot with no warning — this includes bare `quadratic`/`cubic` on 2-/3-row frames). A degenerate group in a `groupby` fit, or a degenerate facet panel, still degrades to a NaN range instead of raising. The `Smooth` constructor's `degree` default changed from `2` to `None` so a bare `method="lm"` stays linear. All other existing `lm`/`loess`/`quadratic`/`cubic` output is byte-identical.

- **Breaking:** `mark_line`, `mark_area`, and `mark_ribbon` all render real `interpolate="monotone"` (Fritsch–Carlson), `"basis"` (uniform cubic B-spline), and `"cardinal"` (Catmull–Rom) curves through one shared Rust curve module. Previously `"monotone"`/`"basis"`/`"cardinal"` silently fell back to a straight line on `mark_line`/`mark_area`, and `mark_ribbon` refused any `interpolate=` other than the default outright. An interpolate value outside the seven-name vocabulary (`linear`, `step`, `step-before`, `step-after`, `monotone`, `basis`, `cardinal`) now raises on every mark that accepts the kwarg, via one shared typed error. Two disclosed byte-family changes on charts that already pass `interpolate=` (never on charts that don't): a banded/stacked `mark_area`/`mark_ribbon` under a step variant now steps **both** boundary edges instead of only the top one; and a series whose independent axis isn't the vertical screen axis (`Stack(value_axis="x")`, or any chart under `CoordFlip`) now curves along its true axis instead of always assuming vertical.

- `mark_arc(corner_radius=...)` — previously accepted by the mark-kwargs allowlist but never read — now clips each wedge corner with an exact tangent-circle fillet, clamped per-corner to fit; the same wedge path renders a rounded polar `mark_bar`. `corner_radius=0` (the default) is byte-identical to every arc/polar-bar render from before this change. Interactive-only, disclosed: polar wedge scenes (arc and polar bar) gain a `polar_spans` key per node, so a full-circle wedge (a one-slice pie, a single-group `pad_angle=0` donut) is now hittable at every angle instead of only across a half-ring; static SVG is unaffected.

- a rug `mark_tick(band_size=...)` — one quantitative axis encoded, no categorical channel — is no longer silently ignored. It now scales the rendered tick length by the panel's cross-axis extent (height for an x-rug, width for a y-rug). An unset `band_size` keeps the old fixed length exactly, not the ordinal default of `0.6` (which would turn an untouched rug into panel-spanning bars). Ordinal-tick `band_size` is unchanged, including a strip-plot-style tick that declares an ordinal axis alongside its own quantitative one; only a tick whose x resolves to a quantitative scale while y is *also* separately declared (the boxplot-cap/errorbar-cap idiom, not a rug) keeps its fixed length and ignores `band_size`, by design.

- **Breaking:** `mark_geoshape` with geometry present under any coordinate system other than `CoordGeo`, and `mark_arc` under any coordinate system other than `CoordPolar`, now raise a typed error naming the mark and the required `.coord()` fix — instead of `mark_geoshape` rendering a silently empty panel (axis furniture only, no geometry), or `mark_arc` failing later with a generic missing-channel error that didn't name the real cause. `mark_image`'s existing documented empty-scene behavior under a non-Cartesian coord is unaffected. Interactive-only, disclosed: `fm.layer(cartesian_chart, polar_arc_chart).interactive()` now refuses with the same typed error (the merged-flat interactive lowering drops the secondary layer's own coord, so the arc mark genuinely has none to satisfy its guard by the time it resolves); the identical chart's static `to_svg()`/`to_png()` is unaffected, since the composite overlay tree renders each layer against its own coord.

- **Breaking:** `X(field, bin=True)`/`Y(field, bin=True)` finally render on a standalone chart. Previously the encoding kept pointing at the original, unbinned field after the `Bin` transform replaced the batch schema, so the chart either raised an unknown-column error or rendered garbage. The `Bin` transform gains optional per-bin aggregation of a second column (`agg_field`/`agg`, drawn from the standard aggregate vocabulary) emitted as a new `agg_value` output; the encode path automatically folds `X(v, bin=True)` + `Y("w", aggregate="mean")` into one `Bin` (the "standard histogram pattern" the docs already claimed works). Every rewritten channel's axis title recovers the user's own field name instead of `Bin`'s internal `bin_start`/`agg_value` column names. A layered binned chart also gains an `x2`/`y2` span so binned bars draw their true bin width instead of the mark's default width — the one disclosed byte-family change on charts that already passed `bin=True`. Six new typed refusals replace what used to be either a silent no-op or a confusing downstream error: more than one `bin=` in an encoding; more than one channel carrying `aggregate=`; the same channel carrying both `bin=` and `aggregate=`; a fieldless non-`count` aggregate; a pre-built `bin=Bin(...)` asked to also absorb an opposing `aggregate=`; and an unknown aggregate method name.

- `mark_function(clip=False)` is a real toggle instead of a hard raise. The curve now genuinely extends past the panel edge for out-of-domain evaluation points, via a new per-batch scene-graph clip escape (`MarkBatch.clip`) rendered by both the SVG and WASM walkers. `clip=True` (the default) is byte-identical to every render from before this escape existed.

### Fixed

- **Breaking:** Every value column a statistical/window/flatten/aggregate transform reads now accepts any integer, unsigned, or float dtype (widened to `Float64`), instead of hard-rejecting anything but `Float64` (about 20 transforms, including `mark_errorbar`, `mark_smooth`, `mark_boxplot`/`boxen`/`violin`, `mark_raster`, `mark_swarm`, `transform_regression`/`loess`/`impute`, and `data_bin`/`data_stack`/`pivot`) or silently emitting nulls/NaN/an unsorted result for a dtype it couldn't handle. A dtype it genuinely cannot read now raises, naming the transform, column, and dtype, instead of the two silent failure modes: `transform_filter`/`transform_calculate` over a `UInt8`/`UInt16`/`UInt32`/`Float16` column used to silently evaluate to `Null` for every row (an `Int64` column with identical values worked fine) — the expression field reader now reads every numeric width plus `LargeUtf8`; `transform_timeunit` with an unrecognized unit string used to emit an all-`NaN` column instead of raising; `transform_top_k` with an unsupported `field` dtype used to synthesize a `row_{n}` key per row (silently defeating grouping) instead of raising. `transform_flatten` on a list-of-Utf8 column now emits real strings (it used to emit an all-null `Float64` column for any non-numeric list element type); list-of-int/float widens to `Float64`; a carried (non-flattened) column now preserves its exact dtype via Arrow's `take` kernel, including a `Float64` column's nulls staying null instead of becoming `NaN`. The Python-side `cast_columns_to_float64` shim ahead of `boxplot`/`boxen`/`violin`/`swarm` is deleted — it silently coerced non-numeric-looking columns (including numeric *strings*) before the engine could refuse them by type; the engine's own typed refusal now applies uniformly. Every dtype that worked before renders byte-identically.

- Window `sort=` and `field=` columns accept every numeric dtype, and refuse the rest. An integer, unsigned, float or temporal column in `sort=` used to compare as "equal" and leave the window unsorted, so the guide's own cumulative-sum-over-integer-`day` example accumulated in row order. An integer `field=` used to read as NaN, so `lag`, `lead` and the rolling aggregates returned an all-null column. Both now coerce once per transform. A dtype the window can neither order nor aggregate (`Boolean`, `Binary`, list types) raises a `ValueError` naming the transform, the column and the dtype instead of silently producing an unsorted window or an all-NaN column, and a text column used as a numeric `field=` raises rather than aggregating to NaN. A `field=` is now interned (and validated) even for ops that never read it, such as `row_number` — `{"op": "row_number", "field": "ghost"}` now raises `data_window: column 'ghost' not found` instead of silently ignoring the field.

- Nulls in a window `sort=` column order last. A numeric `sort=` column previously compared a null equal to every other value, which left the sort order dependent on input order and made "tied" intransitive; nulls now order last and tie only with each other. A text (`Utf8`/`LargeUtf8`) `sort=` column changes in the *opposite* direction: a null string previously coerced to `""` and sorted **first** (and made null a peer of a genuine `""`); it now orders **last**, consistent with the numeric case.

- A window frame lying entirely before its own row is now empty. `frame=(a, b)` with a negative `b` (a window that ends before the current row) used to wrap around an unsigned cast and silently aggregate the whole partition. It now selects no rows, so the aggregate is `NaN`, or `0` for `count`.

- `transform_regression(groupby=...)` and `transform_loess(groupby=...)` were declared on the wire and accepted by Python, but silently ignored by the Rust transform — every group's fit collapsed into one global curve, and the output never carried the groupby column, so `color=` against it raised `unknown column`. Both now fit independently per group, appending the groupby column at its original dtype; each group's output grid spans that group's own x-range, matching `mark_smooth`'s existing grouped behavior. Ungrouped output is byte-identical.

- **Breaking:** `transform_impute(groupby=...)` computed one fill value across the whole column regardless of group; it now computes each group's fill value from that group's own rows. A group with no non-null values of its own falls back to the whole column's statistic rather than degrading to `0.0` (which would silently distort the domain) or `±inf` (which would drop the group's rows from the render). `transform_impute(key=..., keyvals=...)` is newly implemented with Vega semantics: within each group (or the whole batch, with no `groupby`), the key universe is `keyvals=` when given, else the distinct `key` values observed across the batch; a key missing from a group gets one row appended with the key and group columns set and every other column null. `keyvals=` without `key=` raises; a `keyvals` element outside the key column's own dtype width (e.g. `3000000000` against an `Int32` key) raises instead of silently wrapping to a corrupted or duplicate key.

- `mark_swarm(size=/spacing=)` and `mark_raster()`'s default `resolution="screen"` were silently inert in every real render — the transform pipeline never received the chart's pixel dimensions, so swarm always used a fixed collision radius **in raw data units** (not pixels) and `resolution="screen"` always produced a fixed 256×256 grid regardless of chart size. Both now respond to the chart's actual dimensions via a pre-layout size estimate — raster's grid genuinely scales up with a bigger panel. Swarm's pixel spread responds **only to its value-axis panel dimension, inversely** (height when vertical, width when `orient="horizontal"`): a longer value axis produces a smaller spread at the same `size=`, since less cross-axis displacement is needed to avoid collisions. The cross-axis panel dimension has no effect. This is an approximation (it excludes axis/legend/facet-strip gutters, unknowable before layout), not an exact match for the rendered plot area — see the dated note in `ferrum-spec.md` §3.3 (Marks) for the full contract. An explicit numeric `resolution=` is unaffected. `mark_raster()`/`mark_violin` charts change by a small amount (the estimate is close to the true panel size); `mark_swarm` charts change **qualitatively**, not slightly — since the pre-fix radius was a constant in the *value column's own units*, a column ranging over thousands produced no collisions at all and the cloud collapsed to a single line, regardless of `size=`/`spacing=`. No other chart's output changes.

- `mark_tick` now renders per-row `color=`/`fill=`/`stroke_opacity=`/`opacity=` encodings — previously every tick rendered in the theme's flat default color and full opacity regardless of an encoded channel.
